#6901. 23 of Heidi Klum’s Most Iconic Halloween Costumes Through the Years

Her severed hand costume is just the latest jaw-dropping one in her history.

No one does Halloween quite like Heidi Klum. Since 2000, the supermodel has been throwing yearly Halloween parties where celebs gather to mingle and have fun. Just as exciting as her annual bashes, however, are the truly over-the-top costumes she dreams up. Each year, Klum arrives in a costume more elaborate and incredible than the last—it’s truly a sight to see.

Ahead of Halloween, we took a walk down memory lane and rounded up some of the legend’s most memorable costume ensembles throughout the years. From adorable recreations of cartoon characters to fantastical superhero outfits, here are some of our favorites.

For her first-ever Halloween party, Klum wore a striking leather outfit that included a black wig, spiked collar, and knee-high boots. Her red lips and voluminous lashes were also nice touches.

The supermodel pulled a Bianca Jagger and arrived at the function in 2001 dressed up as the 13th-century horse-riding legend Lady Godiva. Her all-white ensemble featured a silver sequined bodysuit paired with a long bleached blonde wig.

The stunning look was created by designer Marc Bouwer. Here, Klum smiles during a fitting for the creation.

In 2002, the star decided on an adorable Betty Boop costume with a strappy red dress, red stilettos, elbow-length gloves, and suitably extravagant makeup. Her Halloween party that year was held at Capitale restaurant in New York City.

She’s pictured here posing with her fellow Victoria’s Secret Angel, Karolina Kurkova, while at the festivities.

For her 2003 holiday fête, the supermodel chose a gold alienesque look which showcased a metallic headpiece and gold metal teeth.

Klum showed up to her bash dressed in a witchy red look that consisted of a waist-length red wig, voluminous pointed hat, bone accessories, and plenty of red eye makeup. The party was held at Marquee in New York City that year.

She wore another spooky look for her 2005 Halloween get-together held in Happy Valley, New York. It’s hard to recognize her between the gigantic black wig and the pointy fangs.

The model attended her 7th annual Halloween party dressed as the forbidden apple (snake headdress and apple dress included) from the biblical story of Adam and Eve. Her then-husband, Seal, showed up as Eve.

In 2007, she stole attention at her get-together dressed in a head-to-toe cat ensemble which consisted of a gray leotard, bedazzled collar, and lifelike whiskers. The party kicked off in Los Angeles that year.

She’s pictured here testing the look a few days before the festivities.

A few years later, Klum was photographed on the red carpet walking into her event dressed as a superhero. The super original look showcased towering platform shoes, an elbow-length red wig, and all-over purple sparkles.

Klum shocked everyone when she opted to wear a skintight costume that transformed her into a skinless body. To make the look even more convincing, she was wheeled onto the red carpet on a stretcher with a “surgeon” by her side.

She flashed a smile at the camera while getting fitted a month or so before the event.

#6900. Ransomware Attacks Move Beyond Enterprise Systems to Theaten Employees' Families

Attackers are exploiting enterprises' own artificial intelligence systems and extending pressure beyond internal networks to employees and their families, according to a new Semperis survey of organizations that suffered disruptive ransomware attacks.

In a recent announcement, the company highlighted findings from its 2026 Ransomware Risk Report, which examines AI-related attack tactics, personal intimidation, and recovery preparedness. Censuswide surveyed 1,200 information technology and security professionals across nine countries and eight industry sectors in the first half of 2026.

The sample has an important limit: This year's study included only organizations where at least one ransomware attack caused business disruption. The results describe those victims' experiences, rather than the likelihood that an organization will suffer an attack. The report says that sampling approach changed from previous studies.

Semperis sells identity security, recovery and crisis management products.

The report says 84% of respondents experienced attacks that exploited AI in some way. Its breakdown distinguishes AI-based attacks, reported by 40%, from attacks that manipulated victims' own AI agents or systems, reported by 44%. That distinction matters: The broader figure covers both attackers using AI and attackers exploiting AI deployed inside an organization.

Defensive use of AI also produced mixed responses. Nearly half of respondents, 49%, said they successfully used AI for detection or response, while 23% said their AI tools failed to detect or respond successfully. Those are survey responses about victimized organizations, rather than a controlled comparison of security products.

Chris Inglis, former U.S. national cyber director and a Semperis strategic adviser, described the difficulty of recognizing AI's involvement. "Many attacks are not perceived as being aided and abetted by AI because they're just too slick, too sophisticated," he said in the report.

Data erasure, destruction, or release remained the most frequently reported threat, at 56%. Blackmail, extortion or public shaming followed at 41%, with system lockouts or operational sabotage at 40%. The report also recorded psychological intimidation at 23% and physical threats at 17%.

In an Aug. 20 Dear Colleague Letter from Kirsten Baesler, assistant secretary of the United States Department of Education's Office of Elementary and Secondary Education, ED outlines new guidance to frame the national conversation around the educational value of technology in schools.

Technology directors touch nearly everything that happens in a school system. Here are three strategies these IT leaders can use to navigate their many duties without losing strategic focus, influence, or sustainability.

CoSN, the professional association for K-12 education technology leaders, has released new guidance to help schools navigate the screen time debate and develop technology policies that protect students while supporting learning.

Increasingly, cyber criminals are sharing AI prompts, jailbreak techniques, and access to existing models that can be incorporated into fraud and phishing operations, according the latest AI Threat Report from Flashpoint.

#6897. Maya Hawke’s Guide to Joining a Global Fandom (Again)

The Stranger Things and Inside Out 2 alum thought she knew what it was like to be a part of a major franchise. Then came Sunrise on the Reaping.

It’s Maya Hawke’s day off. But you wouldn’t know it based on what she’s done so far: a few hours of making flash cards for an upcoming project followed by a full slate of meetings, one per hour. Now we’re at dinner in New York City, talking about her role as the brilliant, unconventional victor Wiress in The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping.

Stepping into a beloved pop culture franchise is nothing new for the 28-year-old multi-hyphenate (she played both the smart, sarcastic Robin Buckley in Stranger Things and the voice of the perpetually frazzled Anxiety in Inside Out 2). Neither is existing in a curious state of constant motion. In the past year alone, Maya has released a folk-pop album, Maitreya Corso; filmed and appeared in multiple TV and film projects; served as a Prada brand ambassador; and gotten married to singer-songwriter Christian Lee Hutson.

Yet beneath the impressive velocity, Maya is interested in all the quiet she can get. “I’m working on being a little less busy,” she says. “Doing half as much twice as well is my current goal.” For now, she’s still able to wander museums and walk through New York without the celebrity disguise of sunglasses and a baseball hat. She’s soaking it in because she knows that come this winter, she’ll be under an arena-size spotlight thanks to Sunrise on the Reaping—and that for her, everything might be about to change.

I am the great joiner! On Stranger Things, I was joining a cast that had done the first two seasons. That held a different kind of weight. With this film, everyone had this newness to them. We are all in it together and will sink or swim as a team. There’s something relaxing about that.

Elle’s Effie is amazing. People are going to lose their minds. I was like, Whoa, that’s how you step on set! I had a different amount to work with. Amanda has three minutes total screen time as Wiress, and what we’ve seen of her character is someone who has been transformed by the events that unfold in Sunrise. We don’t know what she was like before that trauma happened.

I read the book a bunch of times. I rewatched the movies. I was deep in the script. Wiress was not a bright-eyed Capitol kid. She’s a tribute fresh off her win, which she accomplished in a really unusual way. She didn’t hurt anybody. Someone who wins the Games without betraying their moral compass is an extremely special person.

How much power corrupts. It’s not the person in power; it’s what power does to the person. That’s a very clear thesis that runs through the whole story. To make something that is both popular and anti-fascist is pretty crazy. What the books and films are doing is like protest art in a pretty explicit way.

I can get really nervous when I realize how much attention is on something like this. If I start looking at whether people are happy about the casting and the discourse that follows, it’s like the Eye of Sauron. I’m working on caring less about that.

1. Maya’s cowriting contributions are also featured on Phoebe Bridgers’s latest album, Lost Weekend.

The scene when she comes out of her coma—the whole crew got quiet; you could hear a pin drop. When nobody breathes, everyone knows that magic is happening. It grounded me to earth like a big tree. It woke me up. Like a little arrow saying, “Go that way.” And I’ve been following it ever since.

In the beginning of my career, when I was balancing fashion and acting, I had a boyfriend once call me an “AMW”—an actress, model, whatever. The “whatever” was so charged. I had some embarrassment around it. And I wanted to be like, “I’m not a model.” But I didn’t like “actress.”

When I go to Milan to work with Mrs. Prada and Raf Simons, I consistently feel good about myself. Even my husband pointed it out. I was recently auditioning for a project where the character needed to be really confident and he was like, “Be who you are when we go to Milan.”

I filmed Hunger Games and One Night Only back-to-back. They each have these two sorts of apocalyptic fascist governments. My joke on the set of Hunger Games was like, “Beetee,2 we have to have sex right now. We’ve got two hours—let’s go!” Government control in your bed? Horrible. The idea of taking it to that degree is just repellent. I want to be able to explore love on my terms. Bedtime TV, sex, kissing, and love. I mean, who would I be without these things? It would be punishment and torture.

2. Beetee Latier, played by Kelvin Harrison Jr., is an inventor and fellow former victor from District 3 who returns to the arena with Wiress for the Third Quarter Quell. Jeffrey Wright originally portrayed the character.

I consider myself a hopeless romantic. The closest we get to magic is what can happen between two people. Until I actually fell in love with my husband, I was falling in love all the time. I fall in love with my friends every day. I love them very romantically, like how can we spend Christmas together romantically and familially? My biggest heartbreaks of my life have been friendships ending.

It hits different because when you fall in love in a romantic way with someone, some part of you understands that there is a chance that it might expire. There are a lot of things that have to go right for it to work. But with a friendship, you don’t go in with that armor up. You go in so vulnerable, and when it doesn’t work, it’s the biggest tears I’ve ever shed. I hesitate to say this because it’s embarrassing, but I talk about my biggest friendship heartbreak from high school at least monthly, probably weekly. It was really formative.

I’m not going to lie and be like, “I never for one second considered it.” I genuinely thought about it. But the only thing I’ve known that I wanted to do on my wedding day since I was a kid was walk with the whole wedding party.

And they were, to their credit, extremely noninvasive. No one saw them. No one was upset by it. No one got hurt. I was kind of happy that those photographs ran in People because I had thought about doing a story about the dress. I worked with Prada, so it felt like a way to honor them without having to go out in public and say, “Look at me.”

I am not egomaniacal enough to think I will ever not be in my post–Stranger Things era. It will always be a beautiful shadow of my life. I will still consider myself to be in the aftermath of these experiences.

Exactly. I will sign posters until the day I die. Happily so.

#6896. Kendall Jenner Recreated a Cult-Favorite ’80s Movie Look for Halloween

With a November 3 birthday, Kendall Jenner started the celebrations early. The model threw a party on October 30 to fête Halloween and turning 31.

On October 31, she shared photos of her costume. The model transformed into Jane Henderson, the character played by Nastassja Kinski in Wim Wenders’s 1984 film Paris, Texas. The model traded her signature brunette hair for a chin-length platinum-blonde bob. She paired the look with a fuzzy, low-back hot pink minidress and sheer black tights. In one photo, she channels a memorable scene from the film, posing at a bar.

For those unfamiliar with the reference, Paris, Texas follows Travis Henderson, a mysterious drifter who reappears after four years away and attempts to reconnect with his young son and estranged wife, Jane. Directed by Wenders and written by Pulitzer Prize-winning playwright Sam Shepard, the film is considered one of the German filmmaker’s most celebrated works. It took home the Palme d’Or at the 1984 Cannes Film Festival and has since earned cult film status. More than four decades later, its imagery remains recognizable to cinephiles and fashion fans alike.

Jenner wasn’t alone in her cinematic costume. Actor Jacob Elordi joined her as Travis Henderson, the character portrayed by Harry Dean Stanton in the film.

While it marks the rumored couple’s first costume together, it’s not the first year Jenner has thrown a birthday party during Halloween weekend. In 2019, she teased that she was “keeping the Halloween birthday party alive for mom” on Instagram.

This year’s bash, which was thrown at Bar Marmont in Los Angeles, had a star-studded guest list. Attendees included Charli XCX, Hailey Bieber, Laura Harrier, and more.

#6894. IDC Report Finds Significant Gap Between AI Spending and Measurable Financial Returns

Nearly 70% of organizations report a significant gap between artificial intelligence spending and measurable financial returns, according to a recent survey by IDC.

The research firm's 2026 Worldwide AI Economics and Cost of Ownership Survey found that 69% reported such a gap, while only 15% had achieved an AI return on investment exceeding 100%.

In a separate May 2026 survey, 73% of respondents identified excessive AI costs as a significant risk.

IDC highlighted the findings in an October 20 analysis examining what enterprise customers expect from AI technology vendors.

Enterprise customers are placing greater emphasis on measurable business outcomes when evaluating AI products, according to IDC.

The firm identified three priorities for vendors: demonstrating financial value, addressing governance and security requirements, and building support beyond IT departments.

The analysis also highlighted the gap between initial agentic AI deployments and broader adoption. Although 80% of organizations have started deploying agentic AI, only 17% are using it across the enterprise.

IDC said evaluating AI investments can be difficult when the technology changes how employees work and how business processes operate.

The firm expects AI infrastructure capacity to catch up with demand around 2029, potentially easing costs. Until then, vendors face pressure to demonstrate the long-term financial value of their products.

AI funding decisions involve a broad range of executives. IDC's September 2026 C-Suite Tech Survey found that CIOs and CTOs were involved in 63% of those decisions, business-function executives in 62%, and chief financial officers in 61%.

A separate IDC survey found that 42% of CEOs planned to hire a chief AI officer within the next 12 months.

Buyers also reported concerns about the products and terms vendors offer. Limited integration with existing systems, insufficient model and data transparency, and concerns about vendor lock-in were each cited by 34% of respondents.

Overpromising and underdelivering, along with unclear pricing models, were each cited by 31%.

IDC said vendors need to substantiate financial returns and address security and governance requirements while working with decision-makers across IT, finance, and other business departments.

For more information, go to the IDC blog.

In an Aug. 20 Dear Colleague Letter from Kirsten Baesler, assistant secretary of the United States Department of Education's Office of Elementary and Secondary Education, ED outlines new guidance to frame the national conversation around the educational value of technology in schools.

Technology directors touch nearly everything that happens in a school system. Here are three strategies these IT leaders can use to navigate their many duties without losing strategic focus, influence, or sustainability.

CoSN, the professional association for K-12 education technology leaders, has released new guidance to help schools navigate the screen time debate and develop technology policies that protect students while supporting learning.

Increasingly, cyber criminals are sharing AI prompts, jailbreak techniques, and access to existing models that can be incorporated into fraud and phishing operations, according the latest AI Threat Report from Flashpoint.

#6892. Whitney Peak Is Not Online But About to Be Everywhere

She doesn’t read the comments, doesn’t think of herself as a public figure, but still gets followed through airports by screaming teens. Now, as Lenore Dove Baird in The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping, she’s bracing for a fandom she can’t fully evade.

Whitney Peak was walking home late at night, headphones on, when she saw the fight. A group of people were arguing loudly across the street, and things were getting physical. She ducked in and out of a bodega and was steps from her front door when somebody grabbed her arm. It was one of the women who’d been shouting down the block, now yelling in Whitney’s face.

“You’re that girl from that movie! Can I get a photo?” Everyone crowded into a group selfie, recounts Whitney now, smiling into a cup of coffee. “And then they all went back across the street and continued the fight,” she says.

Whitney, now 23, first broke out in HBO Max’s 2021 Gossip Girl reboot with a lead role as scholarship student Zoya Lott. She went on to play the teenage Becca in Hocus Pocus 2 and has since starred alongside Sabrina Carpenter in the singer’s “Nonsense” video and started a partnership with Chanel. It’s a résumé that means sometimes people pause fistfights to speak to her, and sometimes packs of adolescents charge after her through airports.

“There was a group of 13-year-old girls, and they were adorable,” she says of another recent encounter. “I was walking ahead of them with my headphones on and they ran up behind me, so all I could feel was the stomping.”

In hindsight, it’s all been a preview of the fandom Whitney is about to meet with The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping. In the film, she plays Lenore Dove Baird, a fierce, musical girl whose great love is Haymitch Abernathy. (Fans know Haymitch as Katniss Everdeen’s weary mentor, but in this story, he’s a teenager who is separated from Lenore Dove when he’s forced into being a tribute for the 50th Hunger Games.) But despite all the excitement around the movie—and Whitney’s nearly half a million Instagram followers—she says she’s not super online, and she insists, “I don’t carry myself as a public figure. I never think about my career as a whole—I feel like I just got here.”

Even so, the fuss over this latest role will undoubtedly still shape her life in some way. Maybe that’s the strange bargain of playing characters people love: You can choose not to live online, but the work follows you into the world.

No, I met her for the first time two months ago on the street. We live in close neighborhoods. She was just walking by, and I was like, “Rachel??”

She was like, “This is crazy how we’re meeting.” I asked, “How about lunch or something?” but she was running back home. When the casting announcement came out, she sent me a DM and put out a really sweet message.1 Her saying that on the internet was big, because obviously people love her movie and The Hunger Games. It was very generous of her and speaks levels to her character.

1. Rachel tagged Whitney in a post where she wrote, “I know she’s gonna do the Baird name proud.”

Rachel and I were sending texts back and forth about that. I was like, “It never occurred to me that we looked alike.” But seeing the side-by-side pictures, I was like, “Wow. We really could be family.” It’s encouraging to me that I have her preparing me for the overwhelm that comes with joining such a franchise. She didn’t want it to be scary but wanted me to know it can get pretty intense. I get emotional because I hadn’t really let myself think about it, and then talking to her, it comes to the surface. I’m such a fan of hers. And this freaking cast is so insane.

We met in person for the first time at a chemistry read,2 and I walked in trying to be normal, and then Joe just came in like, “What’s up?” and put his phone and his cigarettes on the table, totally relaxed. It was immediately easy. The next time I saw him, we were shooting in Germany and he drove by our hotel in this black sprinter van, screaming my name out the window.

Our actual first scene together, we shot in this beautiful countryside field, and I remember being really, really nervous. But by the end, we were just playing. It made everything concrete between our characters and the two of us. Joe is such a goofball and really loves to laugh, which is one of my favorite traits in people. You can’t be self-serious when everybody is keeping it light. It’s kind of like, Tap in.

2. Whitney’s team has a running bit about her post-audition self-assessments: If she texts, “I bombed that,” they assume she got the job. The one time she felt an audition went really well, she didn’t get it. “I really don’t think it’s humility,” she says. “I have no fucking clue what went on in there.”

We did a lot of prep. I remember we would hang out pretty much every single day and just run the scenes over and over.

It’s one step at a time for me. I don’t really read reviews or comments or messages, and I’m not online very much. Obviously, you still care what people think.3 You want people to like your work. I feel like I live a pretty chill life4 considering my profession, but I do lean into it, and I can be freaked out and overwhelmed but still do it. And I’m going to tell you with this one, I was really freaked out.

3. Whitney also finds it difficult to watch herself perform. Once a project is finished, she says, “It’s no longer yours,” and she prefers to let it go. She’s making an exception for Sunrise on the Reaping though: “There’s so much of it that I didn’t see.” She is excited to watch but expects to be covering her eyes when her scenes come on.

4. Her approach to staying chill is refreshingly simple: “When I’m not working, I really prioritize being in my life and in my relationships.” Her non-Panem agenda includes reading and catching up with family and friends, two of whom are expecting a baby. Whitney is already halfway through knitting some tiny yellow clothes.

My PR team would probably be like, “No, shut up,” but I was! I don’t mean to be self-effacing or self-deprecating. I’m just being completely honest that I felt way in over my head with this. When something just feels so much larger than anything you could have conceived and it’s something people love, with a character who is really important to the story, and it’s just little old me doing all of that…I had to not think about it and just do it.

I let myself make a fool of myself. I had to sing in front of people, and I did it really badly just to get that out of the way and get a good laugh out of everybody. And then I felt like I could just kind of go. I was also listening to a lot of music: Irma Thomas, Bettye Swann, Kendra Morris.

I’m a huge music listener. But I wouldn’t say that I can actually play. I have a keyboard and a guitar that I fiddle with. I just kind of strum and see what sounds nice. I learned how to play the accordion...well, I learned how to play one song on the accordion.5 I’m a perfectionist, so I was really overdoing it. It’s all I wanted to do every single day.

5. There’s sweet irony in saying, “I can’t really play any instruments,” and then rattling off three you own and enjoy. But this is on-brand for Whitney, who is humble about her many talents.

Her relationship to the people she loves. We both value that a lot, and we both value community a lot. And there are so many ways I want to be more like her. She’s very outspoken, and she’s very, very self-possessed. There’s no second thought in her mind before she jumps into action. She trusts her gut, and she just cannot stand to watch anybody be in pain or be treated poorly. And she takes up space! I tend to retreat into myself. I’m just more of a thinker than a talker.

I’ll just say this: Bring a box of tissues.

#6890. Report: Global Ransomware Attacks Rise 23% in Q3

Global ransomware activity increased 23% in the third quarter of 2026, with organizations in North America and Europe accounting for the largest share of attacks, according to cybersecurity firm NCC Group.

The company's Cyber Threat Intelligence Report Review of Q3 2026 recorded 2,865 ransomware victim claims, up from 2,325 in the previous quarter.

Despite the quarterly increase, attacks declined 26% month over month in September to 821. North America recorded the most attacks that month with 390, followed by Europe with 211.

The industrial sector accounted for 29% of quarterly attacks, while Qilin remained the most active ransomware group for the sixth consecutive quarter, recording 365 victims. The Gentlemen followed with 356, and INC Ransom ranked third with 109.

The report highlighted a July cyber attack against a small UK power generation facility that forced operations to stop for approximately four days.

Although the incident did not affect the national electricity grid, it demonstrated how cyber attacks can disrupt physical infrastructure.

The security researchers said the incident was consistent with techniques previously associated with Iranian-affiliated campaigns, including attacks against internet-exposed industrial controllers.

However, the UK government has not formally attributed the attack, and the techniques used remain unconfirmed.

The report also identified growing security challenges as organizations connect traditional IT networks with operational technology (OT) systems for remote maintenance and centralized management.

The firm recommended network segmentation, stronger privileged-access controls, and continuous monitoring of OT environments.

The research found that more than 40 vulnerabilities affecting enterprise applications, VPN appliances, e-mail gateways, and management platforms were added to CISA's Known Exploited Vulnerabilities Catalog during the quarter.

The report examined Gunra, a ransomware-as-a-service operation that has targeted 53 victims across multiple industries as of Q3.

According to the cybersecurity company, Gunra exploited vulnerabilities CVE-2024-55591 and CVE-2025-24472 in internet-facing Fortinet devices to gain access to corporate networks.

The group also used stolen credentials, session hijacking, and administrative tools to move between systems before deploying ransomware.

Other emerging ransomware groups included Deadlock and Storm, with the latter claiming 78 victims by the end of September.

Researchers also observed initial access brokers advertising compromised VPN, Remote Desktop Protocol, and SSH access, alongside access to cloud identities, source code repositories, and security management platforms.

The quarterly review also highlighted BraZetsu, an AI-enhanced Windows malware framework that evolved into a specialized initial access toolkit.

According to the report, BraZetsu uses AI models to automate victim profiling, analyze compromised environments, and identify valuable corporate systems.

The framework searches for enterprise software, financial data, digital certificates, and cloud credentials, helping operators identify compromised access that can be sold to other cyber criminals.

The cybersecurity firm also identified risks involving legitimate AI agents, including prompt injection, excessive permissions, and unintended access to external systems.

The report cited incidents involving U.S. and Australian government systems as examples of agents accessing resources beyond their intended scope.

Researchers warned that these incidents do not necessarily involve malicious intent but can create consequences resembling conventional cyber intrusions.

For the full report, visit the NCC Group site.

In an Aug. 20 Dear Colleague Letter from Kirsten Baesler, assistant secretary of the United States Department of Education's Office of Elementary and Secondary Education, ED outlines new guidance to frame the national conversation around the educational value of technology in schools.

Technology directors touch nearly everything that happens in a school system. Here are three strategies these IT leaders can use to navigate their many duties without losing strategic focus, influence, or sustainability.

CoSN, the professional association for K-12 education technology leaders, has released new guidance to help schools navigate the screen time debate and develop technology policies that protect students while supporting learning.

Increasingly, cyber criminals are sharing AI prompts, jailbreak techniques, and access to existing models that can be incorporated into fraud and phishing operations, according the latest AI Threat Report from Flashpoint.

#6883. Stephen Graham’s Wife Would Like Him to Stop Following Strangers (Literally)

The Adolescence star plays an alluring serial killer in Nocturne. But his own fixation on studying people often leads to awkward encounters. “It was a lot easier when my face wasn’t as recognizable as it is now,” the actor tells Esquire.

Stephen Graham can’t help himself. The Emmy-winning actor doesn’t mean any harm. He just gets fixated on someone, and then...

“I watch people,” he says. “I used to watch people in train stations. Watch walks. And watch how they behave. Watch their mannerisms.” He thinks of this as ongoing performer training. “I’ll store it for later in a Rolodex in me mind,” Graham says in his lilting Liverpool accent, tapping his temple. “You absorb as much as you can. Then you just allow these things to come out.”

Graham plays a charismatic serial killer in the new Apple TV series Nocturne, and while he obviously doesn’t have the character’s murderous tendencies, the two do share a fascination with silently studying those around them. “It was a lot easier when my face wasn’t as recognizable as it is now,” the 53-year-old actor adds.

He became a familiar face in his role as Al Capone on Boardwalk Empire. Then with his scene-stealing performances in Martin Scorsese’s The Irishman and as Bruce Springsteen’s volatile father in Deliver Me from Nowhere. Winning Emmys, BAFTAs, and a Golden Globe for his work in front of and behind the camera on the harrowing crime drama Adolescence made it much harder to study people without being noticed. “I still do it now,” he says, before his wife cuts in.

“You get caught a lot now,” she corrects him.

Graham shrugs. “Now I get caught a lot,” he agrees.

It’s a sunny afternoon in the English Midlands, which is where Graham and his wife, Hannah Walters, keep their home. He’s speaking to Esquire via Zoom, his face covered with a scruffy beard and his hair lightly mussed. He’s wearing a bright-blue Teamsters Memorial Picnic T-shirt and a black-rimmed pair of glasses. The actor could pass as either a pub bouncer or a college professor.

“I’m at home, in the kitchen. We’re being very English,” he says. “My wife is making a cottage pie.” He spins the laptop around to face her. “Say hello, darling.”

Walters is also an actor, best known for the film and TV series Boiling Point, and she was a producer with her husband on Adolescence, costarring as the schoolteacher Mrs. Bailey. But she’s not feeling camera ready at the moment, stirring a steaming saucepan full of minced meat as loose locks of blond hair frame her face. “How very dare you…?” she says to Graham in her most haughty dowager voice before waving cheerfully.

She wasn’t officially involved in the making of Nocturne, but Walters was crucial in helping her husband wrap his head around the character of Jurek Walter, a serial killer whose crimes inexplicably continue even though he is locked up in a criminal asylum. He’s a soft-spoken manipulator, a master of analyzing behavior, and he’s able to wheedle into the minds of virtually anyone he encounters. Liev Schreiber plays a lawman on the outside who’s trying to figure out how Walter continues to puppeteer disappearances, and Zazie Beetz is an FBI agent who goes undercover in the detention facility to get close to him, at great personal risk. “And she was like, ‘Well, okay…’ ” The instruction began. “My research was watching crime documentaries with my wife.”

What did she say makes a good killer? “What makes a good killer?” he repeats to the cottage-pie baker.

She laughs off camera. “I don’t think anybody’s a good killer, are they? That’s an ironic statement,” she says. “But what makes a successful serial killer? I think intelligence. There’s a lot of serial killers that aren’t intelligent, but they get found out very quickly.”

“Highly intelligent. Pragmatic,” he says.

“We watched a lot of different people and just nicked little bits from each one,” Graham says. “Then your job as an actor is to try and create somebody who is believable, who is three-dimensional. Looking at the script, I saw the manipulation that Jurek is capable of and how he is a chameleon and he’ll adapt to each situation. So he reads the situation first and plays what he thinks it is that you want.”

That was about getting into Walter’s head. The second part of his prep was getting the right body. “I was getting myself in shape physically, where I looked like I could kill someone with my bare hands,” Graham says, holding his mitts out in front of him. “That was also one of my aims. Then you just build the character together with the director and with the writers.”

Showrunner John Hlavin (The Shield) says a lot of the work was already done by the time Graham arrived on the Pittsburgh set. “I mean, I’m not going to tell Stephen Graham how to do it. You know what I mean?” Hlavin says. “You’re dealing with a top line of professional athlete.” His contribution was making sure that Jurek Walter remained distinct from other infamous screen sociopaths, like Hannibal Lecter, who overpowers people with his erudition and mind games. Jurek Walter wins them over with vulnerability and sympathy.

Hlavin was struck by how quickly Graham could flip into character. “You’ve met him. He really likes to play. He’s very funny. He’s empathetic and personable. He sometimes would come to set singing, and it would echo around,” Hlavin says. “Then he snaps in. I would rehearse with him, we could be going over lines or making adjustments, and he would drop into character and I would be terrified. He’d go from friendly Stephen, chatting about the Steelers game he was invited to, to Jurek Walter.”

Graham gets physically animated when he talks about playing the role, like a little kid opening up a gift box that has something very dangerous inside. While his characters all have different mannerisms and voices, they often share a common trait: intimidation.

Maybe it’s his stocky, bruiser build that gets him cast in these roles, or maybe the cheerful Liverpudlian is just especially good at suppressing his own upbeat nature. He admits to getting a little overeager, especially when it comes to studying people in the wild.

That’s why it’s ironic that his wife, who was so helpful in guiding him to find the character of Jurek Walter, is so determined to hold him back from getting too close to passersby who intrigue him. “We were in London about two weeks ago, and some fella just started walking,” Graham says, rolling his shoulders back and forth. Imagine a person who moves like a too-tall stack of dishes.

“We both looked at him and she grabbed me by the arm and went, ‘Don’t,’ ” Graham says. “Because I’ll follow his walk, I’ll do his walk. I went, ‘But it’s a great walk!’ And she went, ‘Don’t you dare. Don’t you dare.’ I was like, ‘Arrgh … okay.’ ”

Not only is it awkward when the person spots him, but now that he’s renowned around the world, there are public-relations concerns to think about. His wife speaks up from across the kitchen: “Stephen Graham Followed Me into the London Underground—Headline!”“We’re influenced by so many different things,” Graham says. “Do you know what I mean?”

So if you find yourself on the street someday, being watched by someone who looks like the Emmy winner, don’t be alarmed. But you might see your distinctive walk end up onscreen someday.

#6882. The Best Rhode Skincare Products, According to Experts

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

I’ve tried every single one of Hailey Bieber’s Rhode products, and these are my all-time favorites

The Hailey Bieber effect is real—from inspiring others to wear pimple patches on their feet to creating a chrome nail craze. If you are staying up to date with her, then you likely already know that Hailey Bieber sold her brand, Rhode, to e.l.f. Beauty for a whopping $1 billion, just a short three years after she launched the brand. Since the sale, the brand has only accelerated, creating viral product after viral product.

While a billion dollars is a lot, the sale doesn’t come as much of a surprise when you consider the popularity of the products. The formulas are fan favorites among those on TikTok, as well as among the Bazaar team. In a sea of celebrity brands, Rhode stands out, partly because Bieber enlisted the help of skincare professionals like cosmetic chemist Ron Robinson, who created his own line, BeautyStat, and New York dermatologist Dhaval Bhanusali to help formulate the products.

“My ethos behind the brand is that we want to make one of everything,” Bieber told Bazaar when the brand launched in 2022. “One really good moisturizer, one really good hydration serum, one really good lip product. I believe in curating your essentials in your closet the same way I believe in curating your essentials in your skincare.”

Rhode now has seven skincare formulas (including the latest Glazing Mist launch) and has even expanded into making creamy blushes, plush lip tints, and shaping lip liners. But the skincare offerings are still at the core of Bieber’s brand. Ahead, our favorite Rhode skin products that are worth the hype and deliver on the promise of Hailey Bieber-worthy glazed doughnut skin.

Rhode’s Glazing Milk is the product that converted me to a Rhode stan. While the milky essence category continues to grow, Rhode was one of the first Stateside brands to jump on the bandwagon.

The Glazing Milk has a lightweight yet creamy consistency. The ingredient list is like a vitamin shot for your skin, featuring a trio of ceramides, beta-glucan to calm the skin, and a blend of magnesium, zinc, and copper that protects your barrier. It’s the perfect hydration base layer for your skincare routine or can be mixed with a foundation to create a DIY skin tint.

It sounds gimmicky that this mask can depuff and sculpt the face, but the caffeine- and peptide-powered formula truly leaves skin firmer, tighter, and brighter in under 10 minutes.

The Peptide Glazing Fluid was one of Rhode’s first products, offering a glimpse into Hailey Bieber’s plumping skincare routine. While hydrating serums sometimes conjure thick, gloopy textures, this formula is more like a light gel that instantly sinks into the skin. It gently hydrates skin, leaving a subtle glow. My skin drinks up this serum, and it’s the perfect layer under makeup or as an extra layer of hydration and plumping before bed.

There’s a reason this cleanser won a Harper’s Bazaar Skincare Award, and why it’s become my go-to cleanser. With most face washes, I still feel dirt, makeup, and grime left on my face even after a thorough scrub, but the lather on this balm-to-foam cleanser is unmatched—it takes the day off in one fell swoop. Pineapple is the star of the show in this cleanser—specifically pineapple enzyme, which sloughs off dead skin—while polyglutamic acid and green tea extract hydrate.

The Peptide Lip Boost, a nourishing lip mask from Rhode, may be our favorite lip formula from the brand. It’s thicker than the Peptide Lip Treatment and feels more like a hefty balm, providing extra hydration and cushion. Plus, it leaves behind a very slight tingle, which feels more refreshing than painful and plumps the lips and softens fine lines.

Ever since Rhode launched its cooling and depuffing eye patches, they have been staples in Bazaar editors’ routines. The patches instantly smooth and depuff the under-eye area, and they don’t budge, so they are perfect for multitasking.

The brand’s latest skincare launch is like an SOS button for parched, irritated skin. A thicker, more hydrating iteration of the brand’s Barrier Restore Cream, the Barrier Butter harnesses the power of hyaluronic acid (utilizing five different molecular weights of the ingredient) and ceramides, along with nourishing, rich murumuru butter, to lock in moisture.

I love using the rich, buttery cream as the last layer of my nighttime routine, but also on any dry patches from head to toe. Hailey Bieber told us she uses the Barrier Butter everywhere—from her neck and chest to her hands and even her arms.

The lip balm that started it all, Rhode’s Peptide Lip Treatment went viral when the brand first launched, and for good reason. While the texture of this skincare-infused treatment feels more like a gloss than a traditional treatment balm, it leaves lips with a high-shine finish that also plumps courtesy of peptides. The treatment has now expanded into tinted versions and has even inspired other peptide-forward lip products, such as the Peptide Lip Shape liner.

The brand’s newest launch is a hydrating face mist designed to refresh the skin and add a glow with a single spritz. While I haven’t gotten my hands on the Glazing Mist yet, it has all the hallmarks of a great mist, including a superfine mister. The formula boasts a ceramide trio to improve the skin barrier, ectoin to keep skin hydrated throughout the day, and panthenol to soothe skin.

Many moisturizers on the market contain actives such as acids or retinol, but Rhode’s face moisturizer has zero actives—just ingredients that focus on calming, softening, and hydrating the skin. When my perioral dermatitis flares up, I reach for this lightweight yet moisturizing formula, which is filled with squalane, peptides, niacinamide, shea butter, and açaí.

While Rhode wasn’t the first to jazz up pimple patches, these 100 percent hydrocolloid patches stay in place while healing and protecting breakouts. You can also use them on blisters, as demonstrated by Bieber.

#6880. The Best Celebrity Halloween Costumes of 2026

The Halloween festivities started early for celebrities. On October 20, Kim Kardashian posted one of the year’s first high-profile costumes, revealing her transformation into her 13-year-old daughter North West, who has embraced an edgier, punk look as a musician. “Halloween 2026🧟‍♀️,” she captioned the post. (West hasn’t shared a public reaction to her mother’s homage just yet.)

Victoria Justice, meanwhile, hosted one of the first parties of the season on October 13. She threw an event called “Love Zombie: The Resurrection” at Say Yes in Los Angeles.

As Halloween got closer, celebrities continued sharing looks, with some of the most extravagant costumes ultimately being unveiled on Halloween night.

Some of the stars’ ensembles were months in the making. In 2022, Heidi Klum, who debuts elaborate costumes at her famous Halloween party each year, told People that she’d start planning her look for the following year on November 1. “When I wake up tomorrow, I start thinking of the next [costume],” she said. Her 2025 Medusa costume took six months to create. Similarly, this year’s gnarly idea was concocted last year and, she shared, took “many, many months” to make.

Some designers work with a much tighter turnaround. In 2017, Yousef Aljasmi told ELLE that he had less than two weeks to make Kardashian’s Madonna costume, a recreation of the singer’s 1991 Academy Awards dress by Bob Mackie. “Once we were confirmed for the job, we had about 10 days to create Kim’s costume,” he explained. “After two fittings, Kim’s costume was perfected.”

#6878. Your Next Signature Scent Should Be Coffee-Inspired

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

These 11 colognes will inspire you to switch up your rotation.

I am not a coffee drinker. Never have been. The taste is too bitter for my liking, and that much caffeine will just keep me up all night, no matter what time of day I drink it. And coffee breath . . . But the smell of an actual cup of coffee? It’s divine. Rich. Sophisticated. And perfect for personal fragrance.

Perfumers include coffee notes in fragrance by using gourmand accords made from roasted coffee beans, espresso extracts, or aromatic molecules that mimic those smells. If you read through the scent notes on your favorite coffee cologne, you might notice something called “Coffee CO2 extract”—that’s a concentrated aromatic and bioactive oil created by passing supercritical carbon dioxide through roasted coffee beans. Pretty cool, right?

But coffee notes don’t always smell the same. Roasted coffee beans or espresso can smell dark and dry with a slightly bitter and earthy quality, whereas sweeter coffee notes like mocha and latte scents can be warm, milky, and rich. It also, of course, depends on what other notes are mixed in, from woody and earthy scents to sweet, gourmand ones. To give you an idea of the range, we rounded up the best coffee-scented colognes out there.

You’ll see plenty of pricey bottles on this list. We will get cerebral about perfume—men’s and women’s is a dumb distinction for scent. We will try to push you toward something interesting. But before all that, we have to pay respects. You might call Polo Red, first released in 2013, a garden-variety mall fragrance, something catering to the everyman. That is not our opinion. For us, considering the price point, it’s a modern classic.

Polo Red is a bit more interesting than your typical guy fragrance. It blends fresh, fruity notes of grapefruit, lemon, and cranberry with warm, rich notes of redwood, amber, and coffee. Add in earthy sage and woods and you get a really interesting combination that goes from bright and airy to deep and sensual thanks to the base notes of coffee, which blend beautifully with the warm woods and amber.

As always, Loewe delivers on its luxury fragrance—even the bottle looks like something you’d find in a museum. Like Polo Red, Loewe’s Wild Coffee brings together fruity notes and coffee, but instead of relying on light citrus fruits, it has notes of ripe mango, bitter orange, guava, and passion fruit, which provide a juicy, tropical backdrop for the rich coffee, amber, and tonka bean to come through.

It’s ridiculous how good Bois Corsé is. It’s a simple fragrance that captures “the textured outer layer of a tree.” Meaning, bark.

The notes are coffee, sandalwood, and tonka bean. The coffee is deep and chocolatey. The sandalwood gives it an earthiness, a bit of air and smoke. The tonka bean grounds it all in a warm, user-friendly base. It’s a masterclass, really. Straight down the middle and sure to get compliments

The coffee note is not as pronounced here, as this is a busy fragrance. You get Davana on top—an herb from the southern portion of the Indian subcontinent, which in perfume form presents green and herby but also a bit boozy, like a nice cordial. Like in most of these other scents, there’s a warm tonka note that pairs with the coffee. The oud is not a boring smoky presentation, but a pretty earthy one.

The overall experience is, as the name might suggest, a busy street corner in a tropical climate. There’s a lot going on, but it’s all working well together—a really captivating scent.

In September, DedCool launched its new viral fragrance, Mocha Magic. It’s a gourmand through and through, but it creates some intrigue by contrasting bittersweet notes of espresso, mocha, and bourbon with more classically sweet notes of cream, biscotti, toffee, and vanilla. In a word: Mmmm.

Speaking of gourmands, Maison Margiela’s Replica collection includes the perfect sweet and spicy take on coffee. It emphasizes the scents of warm coffee, milk mousse, and sweet vanilla at its base, but before we get there, it’s actually quite fresh and uplifting thanks to lemon, lavender, and spearmint. So you get a bit of citrus and herbal freshness before leaning into the rich gourmand of it all.

Dolce & Gabbana’s Devotion for Men will surely turn you on to coffee scents. Warm, sensual, and woody, it highlights herbal lavender, dark coffee, and smooth woods, with an earthy backdrop of oakmoss. It’s a solid, elegant pick for a nice dinner or an evening out.

Now here’s a combination you probably weren’t expecting: refined rose and spicy coffee. As it turns out, light florals go really nicely with black coffee, and it’s a cool contrast you really don’t find anywhere else. And, in classic Tom Ford fashion, there’s a bit of smoky frankincense at the base that gives the fragrance a dark and sexy twist.

Coffee and woods make sense together, and when notes of roasted coffee beans are combined with a variety of different woods, it makes for something smooth and sophisticated. That’s what Roja does here. The nutty side of coffee is brought out by notes of cinnamon, pistachio, and caramelized nuts, while cedarwood, sandalwood, oud, and dry woods emphasize its deeper, drier side.

Inspired by the Italian dessert affogato—hot espresso poured over vanilla ice cream—Mancera’s Amore Caffe blends delicious gourmand notes of amaretto liqueur, vanilla ice cream, speculoos, and brown sugar with black coffee to give it that addictive bittersweet quality.

Sweet, spicy, and woody, Kilian’s Intoxicated is another beloved coffee scent, though it’s definitely a strong one. So if you want something bold, this is a good pick. The unique mix of dark mocha coffee with green cardamom, cinnamon, nutmeg, and vetiver gives it a rich, sexy, and spicy intrigue.

#6876. Heidi Klum’s Halloween Costume Just Potentially Spoiled ‘The Batman Part II’

Heidi Klum once again came through with a Halloween costume that was equal parts inventive and deranged—and while nothing will ever top the time she showed up as a worm, dressing up as your own severed hand? It certainly comes close. However, behind-the-scenes photos showing how Heidi’s costume was made seem to have potentially spoiled the upcoming Batman movie, The Batman Part II. Which, whoops!

Heidi’s costume was made by Mike Marino and the team at Prosthetic Renaissance, who are working on the new Batman movie. As Marino put it to W Magazine, “To be honest, I’m a comic book nerd, so the fact that I’m working on [The Batman: Part II] is a dream scenario. We’re doing some really cool makeup in this new movie, so that’s the dream right there.”

There’s a box marked “Zsasz” (a Gotham killer) by a cast of what appears to be Sebastian Stan’s head, a cast of Brian Tyree Henry, which hints at him being Two-Face, as well as a box marked OWL 3 (which hints at the secret society the Court of Owls playing a role in the movie). Of course, it’s possible that these casts and boxes have nothing to do with The Batman: Part II, or—if you feel like being a conspiracy theorist—that they were purposefully left in the background to get the rumor mill going. Either way, I’m invested.

#6874. 6 Safety Razors That’ll Make You Swear Off Multiblade and Electric Shaving

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

One blade gives you a smoother shave and is better for your skin.

If you shave your face with any sort of frequency (daily, every other day, weekly), chances are you use a multiblade cartridge razor—a Gillette razor is what smart money would bet on. It’s the most common option for a reason. Cartridge razors are cheap, readily available, and easy to pick up new blades for (which frequently aren’t as cheap). But if you’ve often found yourself suffering from razor bumps, ingrown hairs, or razor burn—all issues that come readily to those with coarse or thick hair—you might need a course correction. That is because multiblade razors tend to cut too close. Or, well ... it’s not that they cut too close; they cut too much. By the time three to five blades track over a hair follicle, it’s underneath the outer layer of the skin. At that point, it’s simple mathematics that some of these hairs will not grow back perfectly straight out of your skin. They get stuck underneath your epidermis and cause those big, fat ingrown pimples.

Out in the world of shaving, there are single-blade razors, safety razors, multiblade cartridge razors, hell, even electric razors. In the past decade or so, traditional single-blade razors, aka safety razors, have enjoyed a bit of a renaissance. These blades are particularly good for men with sensitive skin or that thick, coarse hair. Safety razors, since they have only one blade, are able to shave close to the skin without going overboard. That comes with some risk. It is easier to cut yourself with, since the blade is sharp and unprotected by some of the features—like moisturizing bars—that you find in cartridge razors. There’s also a pretty steep learning curve associated with them, particularly if you come from the cartridge-razor world.

For those who put in the work and get the hang of it, many say a safety razor gives a far superior shave. And they do save you money in the long run. What else are you waiting for? Oh, for me to tell you what the best ones are. Easy enough. For my money, these six are the best safety razors you can buy.

Some might say Bevel was responsible for the safety-razor renaissance of the past decade with the release of this razor (one of its very first products). The thought behind this design, and the reason many people prefer safety razors, is that they don’t cut as close to the skin as multiblade razors, which helps prevent razor bumps and ingrown hairs. That’s a great thing for men with coarse and/or curly hair, which tends to form ingrowns more easily—and it’s also great for anyone with sensitive skin.

This handy razor is on the weighty side at over four ounces, which can be a good thing for a safety razor, and the sleek design is easy to understand and use. The double-edged blades are easy to replace using the screw top. Whether you’re a safety-razor beginner or expert, this is always going to be a good pick.

If you’re new to safety razors, investing in an expensive one might not be your top priority. This budget-friendly razor is all you need in order to get the hang of safety-razor shaving. It has a traditional screw-top design that uses the same double-edged blades as some of the more expensive handles on this list. The handle has a good heft to it that, combined with an easy-to-grip handle, helps you get used to shaving at a 30-degree angle. The closed-comb design is ideal for beginners and pros alike and delivers a smooth, close shave with minimal risk of nicks and cuts. If you’re a beginner, it’s a great option to learn with. If you’re a pro, it’s a great travel option so you can leave your pricier tool at home.

If you’re starting shaving with a safety razor from scratch, there are a few things to know. The two key elements are the weight of the razor and the angle of the blade. You want a handle that is hefty and feels heavy in your hand, not so light that you’re tempted to move it too quickly over the surface of your skin. The right weight lets the blade do most of the work and allows you to shave your hair with minimal strokes and less risk of nicks. The blade angle should always be about 30 degrees, not perpendicular to your skin, so you usually want to hold the razor at a slight angle.

This well-designed razor from pubic grooming giants Manscaped takes both of those into account. The weight is perfectly hefty, and the blade is actually already designed to sit at a 30 degree angle, so you don’t need to worry as much about how you’re holding it. Both of those things make this razor a perfect jumping off point for those who are coming from the multiblade side.

Safety razors are good for a lot of things, but if you’ve ever used one to try to tackle a few days’ worth of stubble, you know it’s not always great at powering through a lot of growth—at least not with the risk of nicking or cutting yourself going up exponentially. That’s where this safety razor shines. There are six different adjustable settings that subtly widen the space between the razor and the comb, allowing for thicker and coarser hair to be shaved more easily. If you shave every day, you’ll likely want to keep it on the lowest (zero gap) setting, which is basically your classic safety-razor setting, but if you tend to go a few days between shaves, change the setting to allow you to shave your stubble more easily with fewer strokes.

Sometimes you gotta go with a classic. Merkur has been making razors in Germany for over 125 years, and you can’t argue with that pedigree. This traditional safety razor has a traditional screw-top design and a weighted head that allows you to get a silky-smooth shave pretty much immediately. There aren’t any newfangled features in this thing, but that’s kind of the point. The nonslip coating and the perfectly weighted head, as well as the closed-comb design, make this the ideal short handle traditional razor.

Up until this point, I’ve been talking about closed comb razors. The difference, as you can see, is the exposed teeth of the blade on this style, making it sharper but also more of a risky proposition. Knowing your safety razor basics is a must, but if you have the technique down, this is a way to tackle longer or coarser beards.

Don’t get me wrong, this is an expert move. For those of us who grow facial hair faster and can never get a shave as close as we want before it starts growing back, the open comb razor is one way to achieve this. The cleanest shave of your life won’t just feel great, many say it will make your hair grow back slower.

Whether or not you’re experienced with safety razors, there are a few things to take into account when choosing the right one for you.

Weight: You’ll notice that most safety razors are way heavier than the drugstore cartridge razors you may be used to, and it’s not just because they’re made of metal. That’s by design. Safety razors tend to concentrate their weight in the head, which allows them to use gravity to give you a smooth shave without applying too much pressure (which can lead to nicks and cuts). Not all safety razors are the same weight, though. Ultimately you want to find a razor that is heavy enough to give you a good, close shave but not so heavy that your hand gets tired halfway through the process.

Blades: Most safety razors will use a traditional double-edged blade. This allows you to use both sides of the blade to get a little more usage out of it. Some razors will use a single-edged blade, usually called an injector blade, which is often thicker and more durable than its double-edged counterparts. Double-edged blades are preferable, but whichever you choose, make sure refills are readily available, as you’ll need to change the blade every few uses. (Most safety razors, especially those that use double-edged blades, can be refilled with any brand of blade.)

Razor Head Type: Speaking of changing the blades, safety razors typically come with two types of head. The most common is screw-off, where you unscrew the head of the razor in order to change the blade. The downfall of these is that while they’re easy in theory, it means the head comes in multiple parts that must be unscrewed and then screwed back together, making for a somewhat complicated experience, especially for beginners. The other kind, butterfly, pops up in the middle and allows you to drop in the blade.

Open Comb vs. Closed Comb: In this case, “comb” refers to how much distance there is between the razor head and the blade itself. The more open the comb, the more aggressive the shave. Closed comb razors are especially ideal for beginners because they help cut down on the risk of nicks and cuts (as there is no space for skin to get caught). Open-comb razors are ideal for people with thicker beards or longer stubble, as they allow more hair to reach the blade at once, but they carry a higher risk for nicks and cuts.

Adjustable Settings: Some razors will allow you to adjust the settings to take it between a closed and an open comb. Usually this involves different inserts that push space between the blade and the razor head. While not always necessary for everyone, this is ideal if you like to allow time between shaves and sometimes shave with a few days’ worth of stubble or more.

#6872. Gilded Age S4: Louisa Jacobson and Harry Richardson React to Larry and Marian’s “Utter Agony” in Episode 1

The actors unpack the morning-after fallout.

For three seasons, The Gilded Age fans have waited for Larry and Marian to get together. After their engagement went south, it seemed like it might never happen. But in the season 4 premiere, Larry and Marian (SPOILERS AHEAD for Gilded Age season 4, episode 1) finally spend a night together. It had all the right tropes: A broken carriage wheel, no way to get back uptown, two rooms left in the nearest hotel, no pesky chaperones to get in the way. Bu›t the morning after, it’s clear that Marian is devastated by what’s happened between the two of them, and Larry is left reeling as to what he did wrong.

The actors behind the characters, Louisa Jacobson and Harry Richardson, spoke with Cosmopolitan about what went through Marian’s head the morning after, how Larry will try to understand her reservations as the season goes on, and the joy of playing a will-they-won’t-they couple.

Louisa: It's this huge transgression. Premarital sex was a cardinal sin, and it's something that would completely ruin a woman's reputation in society, completely leave her destitute, leave her without marriage options. She would be thought of as impure. We don't live in this context anymore.

It's really hard to deal with the fact that what might have been the best night of her life is something that is so bad because she's internalized that history of shame. He's not going to feel those consequences.

Louisa: Her father lied to her when he died about her situation financially, and he wasn't a very good father. Her mother died very young. And then there's Tom Raikes from season 1, and then the safe, arranged thing with Dashiell didn't work out, then there’s this mistrust with Larry because of the brothel situation. She's just been burned so many times in such an intense way that there's already a lot of trepidation. And there's a lot of pressure on her from society because she's had two failed engagements. The stakes are really, really high right now.

Harry: It takes the whole season. He's really struggling at trying to understand the gravity of what's happened, but he gets a full force realization in the morning after. Louisa was given one of the hardest tasks ever to shift from the most exquisite night of, finally, this couple we've been rooting for to be able to express their love getting together, and then in one cut we go to what is my favorite shot of the whole season, this POV of her in the mirror. My heart broke for her because you see instantly in one look, "Oh, her life is on the line.”

Beyond all of his desires to connect with her, Larry adores her and wants to be with her. He sees the person he loves the most in utter agony. He’s instantly confronting: My immaturity has now put the person who I care most about in a terrible situation.

Louisa: I was trying to figure out, what's she experiencing physically? Not to be TMI, but you know, stuff happens to your body after you have sex for the first time that can be uncomfortable physically, and if you don't know what's going on, you really suffer.

Harry: I feel like life is a continuous expansion of will-they-won’t-they. Am I gonna am I gonna brave up or am I gonna freak out again? We're all trying to expand our heart. We're also going through all this fear and insecurity. It's amazing to play because we get to confront the parts of ourselves that are closed or scared. This show is so beautiful because even though there's so many complications for every character, as it gets more complex, it gets deeper.

#6871. 8 Chocolate Advent Calendars That Make a Great Holiday Gift

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

December has enough obligations. You have gifts to buy, travel plans to make, and the office holiday party your boss put on your calendar. How do you fight back? How do you reclaim a second, even half of a second, for yourself? We suggest starting with an Advent calendar. Specifically a chocolate Advent calendar.

But forget about the flimsy ones you might’ve had as a kid with the same mediocre chocolate behind every flap; we’re talking luxurious chocolate truffles and beautifully designed calendars here. Think colorful, handcrafted chocolates filled with the most delicious ganache and boxes so beautiful you might even want to keep them long after the holidays are over. We do also have a few inexpensive options you can easily order from Amazon, in case you aren’t looking for anything too fancy.

Whether it’s for you, your kids, or another special someone in your life, these chocolate Advent calendars won’t just make their day once—they’ll do it 24 days in a row.

Of all the chocolate Advent calendars we tested, Dandelion’s was the one that truly blew us away with its craft and beauty. Packaged in a round blue box with gorgeous silver holiday-themed designs are 25 small round containers that double as tree ornaments. I don’t even have a tree to put these on, but I kept a few ornaments anyway just because they’re so beautiful. And what’s inside each box is the best part—the chocolates.

Dandelion partnered with 24 chocolatiers from across North America and Japan to create the most beautiful, intricately designed, and—most importantly—delicious chocolate confections. To make the experience even more special, it comes with an illustrated storybook with tales from Nordic mythology and folklore, and you get access to daily videos in which Thomas DuBois, an expert in Nordic astral lore, shares the mythology behind each day’s tale.

Shipping: Available for preorder now, ships in November.

Another chocolate calendar that combines gorgeous illustrations with scrumptious chocolate truffles is the new Enchanted Castle Advent calendar from Compartés. You may already be familiar with Compartés for its famous chocolate bars, so it’s no surprise that the brand’s chocolate truffles are equally as delicious. With flavors like Buttered Croissant, Pistachio Marzipan, and Lemon Olive Oil Cake, each door holds a wonderfully sweet surprise. I had to stop myself from eating them all at once. And if you’re getting it as a gift for a lucky someone, you can add on gift wrap and a hand-tied ribbon for a few extra dollars.

Shipping: Available for preorder now, shipping starts November 2.

We can’t talk about chocolate Advent calendars without mentioning Godiva. You know her, you love her. This year’s Godiva Advent calendar features a range of chocolate treats from dark chocolate with caramel to milk chocolate with ganache to raspberry-filled white chocolate stars. My mouth is watering just thinking about these chocolates! It’s really the best you’ll get for under $50.

Another favorite of the Esquire editors this season is this super cool spinning chocolate calendar from La Maison du Chocolat. Each chocolate hides inside a small box, and each box is arranged around this sculptural piece that blends Parisian art and design. I don’t want to give it all away, but I’ll just say my favorite pieces were the milk chocolate almond-hazelnut praline filled with caramelized sesame and the dark chocolate mini bar with crushed crepe dentelle and passionfruit white chocolate.

Shipping: Available for preorder now, ships in November.

Associate Commerce Editor Bryn Gelbart has already written a whole love letter to his Hotel Chocolat Velvetiser hot chocolate maker, so when we found out Hotel Chocolat makes a whole Advent calendar filled with hot chocolate mixes, we knew we had to try it out. And in a big shock to absolutely no one, we’re obsessed. We’ve been leaning into the season already with hot cocoa at the office; some of our favorite blends include the Black Forest Gateau, Chili, and the Classic 70% Dark Chocolate.

Like Godiva, Lindt is an absolute classic when it comes to chocolate Advent calendars. In fact, Lindt’s Holiday Teddy Bear calendar may be one of the most recognizable chocolate Advent calendars out there—and at just $14, you can get one for everyone in the family. Perfect for the person who appreciates a good ol’ milk chocolate candy.

Forgo your morning cup of coffee and start each December day with a piece of caffeinated chocolate from Awake. Once you get a taste of the Plain Milk, Caramel Milk, Peanut Butter Milk, and Salted Caramel Dark Chocolate bites, you’ll want to have these on hand for your next 3 p.m. slump.

#6869. Calvin Klein’s Hot Streak Continues With Jude Bellingham Fronting Its Iconic Underwear Campaign (Exclusive)

Of course, they’d tap football’s soccer’s finest.

Calvin Klein’s iconic underwear ads have been a hot topic since the ’80s, as some of Hollywood’s hunkiest guys fronted the campaigns on blown-up billboards. Michael B. Jordan, Justin Bieber, Jeremy Allen White, and Bad Bunny have been making the girls, gays, and theys go feral over the years, and its latest campaign star is no different. Now, the legendary fashion brand has met its match in global football star Jude Bellingham.

Aside from being one of the best soccer players in the world (and his friendship with fellow footballer Erling Haaland), the 23-year-old athlete is known for two things: his chiseled bone structure and killer abs. So it’s no wonder CK tapped him as the face of its Icon Cotton Stretch underwear—and Cosmopolitan to share some steamy behind-the-scenes pics from the shoot, lensed by Oliver Hadlee Pearch in Madrid last summer.

“I really enjoyed working on this campaign,” Jude said in a statement sent to Cosmo. “I liked the idea of ‘the icon underneath’—that confidence starts with feeling comfortable in yourself. Calvin Klein is such an iconic brand, so it was great to bring that idea to life in my own way.”

Before Jude’s debut CK campaign was officially revealed on November 2, the brand teased the collaboration with a billboard in one of its “out-of-home” placements in New York City. The photo, which was blown up in SoHo, featured Jude’s signature muscles without showing his face for a full-circle IYKYK moment.

It didn’t take long for Jude’s dedicated fan base to recognize the body that brought him all his accomplishments on the field. The brand also dropped a similar teaser to Instagram on October 27, writing, “The next icon. Coming soon.”

Everyone say, “Thank you, Calvin Klein,” for blessing us with these new pics of Jude!
