#6866. How One Mistake Unleashed a Horde of Night of the Living Dead Bootlegs

A simple copyright error allowed George A. Romero’s zombie classic to enter the public domain. For years, the filmmakers fought to get the rights back.

would you rather accidentally set your arm on fire with spilled gasoline or spend decades grappling with a paperwork mistake? Gary Streiner, who personally experienced both while making 1968’s Night of the Living Dead, says there’s no contest: “Oh, believe me, I would take the burn again.”

Streiner was the sound man on George A. Romero’s classic indie zombie flick, but since everyone on the makeshift Pittsburgh project did multiple jobs, he also helped create visual effects, like the fireball on a porch chair that would later engulf his limb. His brother, Russ Streiner, was another multitasker, playing the taunting brother Johnny in the movie’s opening, and serving as jack-of-all-trades co-producer. Both were loyal Romero confidantes who helped him found Image Ten, the production company that created the apocalyptic undead tale.

So both were among those bitten in the ass when Night of the Living Dead accidentally ended up in the public domain due to a copyright mistake that was anything but simple to fix. Even today, it hasn’t entirely been resolved, which is why there is a right way and a wrong way to watch the movie (at least, if you care about seeing it in the best possible format, while also not picking the pocket of the people who made it.)

Today, Romero is gone, and his debut feature is considered an all-time American classic. Stephen King himself calls it the scariest movie he’s ever seen. But the independent film garnered such success partly because it was so widely and freely shared. The friends and colleagues who helped Romero make Night of the Living Dead have finally figured out a way to pull the movie back into their legal possession. But the freebie versions are still out there, so they are hoping fans will be honest and honorable about the version of the film they choose to play this Halloween.

Don’t worry, they aren’t “coming to get you, Barbara,” as Russ intones in the iconic opening of the movie. They are just, once again, asking nicely.

Basically, look for the Criterion Collection logo. The beloved cinema preservation group helped the Streiners restore the original black-and-white negative for the Museum of Modern Art, and by refining the moody imagery as Romero originally intended, they technically created a “new” work that Image Ten could officially claim as their own. You can find it on the Criterion streaming service, but also HBO Max is among those that license their version of the movie.

“All of it’s licensed now. Now we get paid for it. That’s the point, every time it’s screened on any of those platforms,” Gary says.

“There are other streaming channels that aren’t,” adds Russ. If you don’t spot that big, silver C when Night of the Living Dead starts playing, you’re probably watching a bootleg.

The average joe might not care much whether these aging producers or Romero’s estate get their share, but keep in mind that these were average joes too, not Hollywood big shots. They were working stiffs who had a dream, made something immortal (in many senses of the word), and then got stiffed for real.

“To be honest with you,” Russ begins. “George—and I want to measure my words pretty carefully …”

“He was not a great businessman,” Gary cuts in, not measuring his words at all.

“None of us were great businessmen,” Russ adds with a sigh.

The Streiner brothers were toiling away on local Western Pennsylvania commercials when they helped Romero bring his feature film dream together, and their collective work inspired generations of other nobodies to max out their credit cards, load up on cameras and lights, and make a crazy movie with their friends. “It’s fair to say that without George A. Romero, I would not have the career I have now,” Shaun of the Dead and Hot Fuzz filmmaker Edgar Wright wrote when Romero died in July 2017. “A lot of people owe George a huge debt of gratitude for the inspiration. I am just one of many.”

It’s easy enough to find the right version of Night of the Living Dead, and it happens to be the one that looks the best too. Many of the bootlegs are copies of copies of copies. When the Streiners got MoMA and Criterion to revive a new version of the movie, they went back to the original negatives, part of which they possessed, and some of which belonged to Romero’s cowriter. “We gathered up all of the elements from Russ’s basement and John Russo’s garage, and we trekked out to their facility, and we were all quite amazed at the good condition that everything was still in, not having been kept in absolute ideal conditions,” Gary says.

Now it looks as good as it did when the very first prints were sent out into the world. That also happened to be the moment everything went wrong.

You know how old movies have a noticeable copyright tag in their opening credits, usually right under the title? Today, that’s an affectation, used by filmmakers like Quentin Tarantino and Rian Johnson to conjure a feeling of nostalgia. But once upon a time in real Hollywood, that was actually a legal requirement to establish ownership of a film.

Romero and his team understood this, and had it on their initial cut of the movie. The mix-up happened when they were negotiating to get the zombie picture into theaters and struck an agreement with the indie distribution company the Walter Reade Organization. “That was the beginning of our nightmare,” Russ says. “The movie at that time was called Night of the Flesh-Eaters. They didn’t like the title. They said, ‘We’ve got to come up with something new.’ ”

The executives had a point: Night of the Living Dead has a certain ring to it, so Romero and the Streiners agreed. They left the print in Walter Reade’s hands. “They were in charge of changing the title, but when the title got changed, the copyright was left off,” Russ says. “It became their responsibility to deal with their lab, and the copyright notice for Night of the Living Dead went by the wayside.”

No one realized the mistake until the prints were shipped out, and by that point it was too late. “Not that ownership was ever denied,” Gary says. “There’s never been any, ‘Well, no, you didn’t make Night of the Living Dead.’ We made it.”

Under the United States’ 1909 copyright law, this technical mistake just meant no one was legally obligated to account for tickets or pay them a portion of the sale. “That was the start of it, and we’ve been spending tons of money legally to get this thing on its right feet,” Gary adds.

If that seems both unbelievable and unfair, then welcome to the copyright law revision of the mid-1970s that eliminated this requirement. “It’s totally different now,” says Russ. “As a matter of fact, Night of the Living Dead was one of the reasons that the language for the copyright office changed, because it was preposterous.”

But it was too late for the zombie film and the team behind Image Ten, until the new restoration came about from MoMA and the Criterion Collection. The Streiners learned this lesson the hard way, but they were used to that.

Who could have guessed that a simple line of text could cause so much trouble? Well … who could have guessed that when you pour gasoline on a chair for a zombie attack scene that it could somehow also go wrong.

“George didn’t like the way it was looking, and it took about a half hour for him to reset and get the shot that he wanted,” Russ recalls of the infamous incident. “So the chair was put out, and when it came time to do another take in the new camera position, I had been all over that thing diligently making sure that there were no embers or whatever.”

He didn’t think about what was below the surface. “There was no fire on the chair itself, but the interior workings of the chair, the springs and the metal framework, were still hot,” Russ adds. “And when Gary started dousing it with gasoline—BOOM!—it was like an explosion that set him on fire.”

“I’m literally pouring gasoline out of a plastic jug, and the flame shot right up,” Gary says, mimicking the motion.

Spilled gas on his sleeve meant his whole arm was engulfed. “Fortunately, Bill Hinzman, who was a cemetery ghoul, was actually a volunteer fireman, and he had enough common sense to just tackle me and roll me to the ground,” Gary says.

It was over in seconds, with only relatively minor burns that eventually healed. The copyright conflagration took a lot longer to resolve, but now, finally, they don’t feel so scorched by it anymore.

#6865. Newly Minted Mr. Darcy, Jack Lowden, Responds to James Bond Short-List Rumors

Nothing more iconic than going from Mr. Darcy to 007.

We’re this close to finding out who the next James Bond is, and the short list? Callum Turner and Jack Lowden—the latter of whom is in the middle of a truly iconic run in Slow Horses, not to mention about to play Mr. Darcy in Netflix’s upcoming Pride & Prejudice adaptation.

According to The Hollywood Reporter, “several sources” confirmed that final screen tests for the Denis Villeneuve-directed film were taking place in London last week, which…presumably means Bond has been chosen. Cut to Jack, who was asked about the rumors in a recent GQ interview.

“On this… It’s all very flattering,” he said. “It’s nice to be mentioned—for any role—and I’m just not really going to add to the noise.”

Speaking specifically of his Slow Horses costar Gary Oldman saying he hoped Jack would get the part, he said, “It’s very nice, and Gary’s a sweetheart—it’s particularly lovely, coming from someone like him. But I don’t really listen to it. As a young male actor, generally, the minute you’re not playing a zany artist type, the minute you’re in a suit, people fling it at you.”

Not exactly a denial! Meanwhile, Callum told THR over the summer, “I know as much as you do—really, I know as much as you do. I’ll tell you what’s so funny about the Bond thing: Even your best friends ask you, people text you that you haven’t spoken to for 10 years—and you know nothing! It’s such a weird thing of something happening and nothing happening at all. I genuinely know nothing. I just find it quite amusing.”

Honestly, I’d take either of them as Bond. Or both! Just happy to be here.

#6864. Powder Pink Shirts Are Replacing White Styles for Fall

Worn on the runway and in the front row, pink shirts are taking over.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

At Matthieu Blazy’s triumphant Chanel debut last year, the designer featured a powder pink button-down that was made in collaboration with shirtmaker Charvet. The oversized cotton poplin shirt featured two front pockets, pearlescent buttons, and subtly embroidered Chanel branding at the waist. The commercial (and critical) success of the collaboration led to Chanel’s acquisition of Charvet in July 2026—and the trickle-down effect has been decidedly palpable throughout recent fashion weeks.

Sitting on the star-studded front row at Bottega Veneta, Tyler, the Creator was pictured wearing a pink silk taffeta shirt, which he styled over a white T-shirt with beige chinos and a shearling trapper hat. The shirt was from the Italian house’s SS26 collection and stood out among a sea of neutral outfits. With a host of designer collaborations under his belt and two fashion labels to his name (Golf Wang and Golf Le Fleur), when the multi-hyphenate star co-signs a trend, the masses follow.

Just as pink shirts have been worn on the runway and in the front row, they have been spotted continuously on the street style scene throughout fashion month—and not just in one city but several.

In London, the styling manifested in a mixture of elevated and casual outfits. One show-attendee wore a structured blazer placed over her pale pink shirt, which she styled with tights and pointed-toe heels, while another wore her shirt collar turned up with a bomber jacket, cropped jeans, and ‘80s-inspired aviator sunglasses.

In New York, the pink shirts were slightly brighter in tone, spotted styled open and tucked into tie-dyed denim. The secret to this effortless look was the accessories: choosing to stack a number of necklaces together and finishing off the outfit with dark sunglasses.

As for Milan, the brief couldn’t have been clearer: pink shirts tucked into statement skirts and worn with confidence.

So, if you’re looking to upgrade your shirt game, we’ve done the groundwork and pulled together a concise edit of the six best pink shirts on the market. You’re welcome.

#6862. Is the Fitbit Edge Worth Buying?

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

The future form of the wearable tech is here.

It’s been a pretty big year for Fitbit. In May, the Fitbit Air joined the ever-growing list of no-screen fitness trackers alongside Whoop, and now, not even six months later, I have on my wrist the brand-new Fitbit Edge. It is, as the name suggests, a slimmer fitness tracker. It feels slightly like a step forward, slightly like a step back—a return to form. Let’s say. It’s new, but it’s also a throwback to the slim shape of the (not so) old Fitbit devices like the Charge and Inspire. Importantly, despite being Google’s latest wearable with a screen, it is not trying to be a Pixel Watch. Far from it. Some might complain about what it can’t do, but I’d argue the pared-back nature of the Fitbit Edge is its greatest strength.

At its core, this is the state-of-the-art Fitbit bringing a smooth, responsive modern touchscreen experience to a wearable focused on health tracking. It certainly does more than count your steps, monitor heart rate, and detect REM cycles; the Edge has plenty of smartwatch capabilities. Control your music, check the weather, even read and respond (depending on the phone you have) to messages. But the standard inundation of notifications is replaced by a clean interface that clearly does not like being disturbed. This is the casual fitness tracker for those who value a distraction-free workout.

First things first. I’ve been wearing mine 24/7 for the past couple of weeks, and it’s nothing if not supremely comfortable. Bands are simpler to attach and detach than any other watch I’ve tried, and the new magnet bands are easy to make snug.

From the get-go, the band selection has been excellent. My personal favorite is the magnetic silicone band in Mahogany. It’s got a look that comes off as more upscale than the materials described typically do. Paired with the slim bezel and overall slight shape, it’s a handsome wearable that draws a lot of compliments. I won’t repeat what one friend of mine said, but I’m pretty sure it means she liked it.

The completely revamped Fitbit interface is intuitive and has everything you need just a swipe or tap away by default. You can tinker with the home screen, but having heart rate and total steps, alongside the time and a quick workout start button, displayed by default feels instructional. Everything is readable and I genuinely feel like my mother-in-law could understand how to use it. Like I said, it’s not a Pixel Watch, but it’s certainly taking usability and design cues from Google’s other line of wearables.

As a Fitbit, nearly everything about the Edge is an evolution. The only problem is I’m also comparing it to all the smartwatches out on the market too. I can use literally any other smartwatch in my rotation to tap into my office building with a digital badge, and pay for lunch with Apple Pay or whatever equivalent. With the Edge, no luck on any of these NFC-powered interactions. And because I’m using it with my iPhone, I can read my incoming messages but can’t respond to anything.

On paper, these are flaws, but in reality they allow me to interact with my fitness tracker without getting too distracted. The lack of customizability compared to an Apple or Pixel Watch simply equals less time sitting around and setting up your watch, and hopefully more time going outside and living your life. Which isn’t to say I wasn’t bugged by some of the limitations. The viewable data is not quite as in-depth as some others, and looking at your phone to check your reps sorta ruins the whole distraction-free thing.

In that way, it’s more like a fitness band than a smartwatch. It can track a lot of data on its own, but ultimately the only way to see all of that is to eventually open your phone. For me, that trade-off is worth it to not be getting constant buzzes on my wrist every time I go for a run.

When it comes to fitness tracking, the hardcore still have Garmin. For something more casual, the Fitbit Edge has a great look, and as an Apple Watch user, certain aspects of the simplicity are genuinely refreshing.

#6861. My Two Wedding Dresses Were a Love Letter to My Haitian Roots and New Ghanaian Family

I was looking for just one perfect gown, but what I got was a beautiful story of bridging heritage, family, and belonging. (And of course, two absolutely stunning custom pieces.)

I thought I would cry. There I was perched prettily on a podium in a gorgeous champagne wedding dress and mantilla veil. My mom and three sisters beamed up at me in between their sips of bubbly, awaiting my enthusiastic proclamation that this dress was, much like my fiancé, The One.

A soft violin rendition of an inspirational song slowly crescendoed through the speakers, and it all felt like signs that this was the gown I’d say “I do” in. I stared at myself in the mirror almost blankly, tilted my head, and asked my family, “Really? This is beautiful but is it me?” I tried to find the words to explain to my heart-eyed audience that there were no butterflies, no fireworks, no cinematic overture, no little voice spelling out yes.

I waited, hoping the rush that was supposed to sweep me away was a little off schedule. But...nothing. Isn't this the part where I’m supposed to burst into tears? I couldn’t rationalize how all of the frenzy and booked appointments came down to this level of underwhelm. I left without a dress that day, and even though my wedding was in a mere two months (yes, really), I wasn't panicked—yet.

I know what you’re thinking: “Isn’t it a little late to be wedding dress shopping?” And normally, you’d be right. But after getting engaged in November 2025, my fiancé and I decided to skip the long engagement. There was just one tiny detail: We had two months to plan the entire wedding, which meant finding the dress of my dreams was officially a race against the clock.

Unfortunately, this dress scenario played out several more times. By the time I got to Kleinfeld Bridal, the iconic New York City shop featured on TLC’s Say Yes to the Dress, I was positive I would find the perfect wedding dress there. I felt confident, primed to cancel future dress appointments, telling myself this day would end with the happy tears and excitement that I dreamt of as a little girl playing dress up. My mom and sisters were aligned, and kept their eyes open for any “wow” moments. Then my stylist brought out an every-bride-would-love-this custom Pnina Tornai gown that had a compelling backstory. A sucker for some good lore, but not an emotional buyer, I was okay with not having it. Once again, I left another bridal salon dress-less.

All of this had me perplexed: I loved how I looked in every gown, but it felt like it was something bigger missing and made me consider meeting with a therapist to figure it out. Was it wedding jitters? I dissected myself after every boutique visit, and often wondered, “What’s wrong with me?” Conscious of the clock, part of me wanted to just pick one and keep the peace. Appointment after appointment, maybe I was too difficult to please? But I refused to talk myself into a dress—I am allergic to settling, after all.

I grew up Haitian-American, and in my family, there was a real appreciation for presentation and what you wore. You dressed up for church, celebrations, and even going to the grocery store. Don’t get me started on school picture day. Even in college, while my classmates wore sweats to class, I’d be pumping through campus in heels. My family instilled in me that you showed up looking good because looking good was part of showing up. It was more than superficial; looking good was a way of showing respect, taking pride in yourself, and representing your family with honor. So I wasn't just trying to find a beautiful gown. Before I took on a new last name, filled with new traditions, I needed to recognize myself—where I come from—in it.

Everything shifted on a solo trip to San Francisco. I was luxuriating in bed at the Palace Hotel when a thought landed on me like fresh dew in the morning: I, a Haitian-American fashion girlie, was marrying a Ghanaian artist—I was entering a marriage that brought two cultures, two families, and two creative identities together. What if I wear a Haitian designer for the ceremony, and then honor his culture with a Ghanaian traditional gown during the reception? I thought. Finally, the feeling I had been searching for started coursing through my body.

For my Haitian piece, I began researching and it ended almost instantaneously when I stumbled upon Jean-Ralph Thurin, a Haitian designer I was already following on Instagram. We met years ago during NYFW when I was getting my start in the industry. I reached out and scheduled an appointment for as soon as I landed back on the East coast.

Jean-Ralph’s atelier was elevated, with cream, ivory, and white gowns displayed throughout the luscious showroom; each one reminded me of some special element of Haiti. The loveseats created an intimate, almost familial feeling, like you’d come over to someone’s beautiful living room and settled in to watch a show.

The family vibes only intensified as the appointment went on. Jean-Ralph’s wife, a fellow designer, helped me in and out of dresses, and his daughter was his attendant. It felt like being at my aunt’s house, and suddenly it made sense why none of the other shops worked for me—I was meant to be here.

As my sisters oohed and ahhed at me in a dress that I couldn’t stop smiling in, Jean-Ralph sketched an entirely new option for me in his notepad. It was a rendition of what I had on (which was sadly too big and couldn’t be altered due to the elaborate detailing) and totally me. “I’m inspired,” He told me. “I’m going to design this for you.” I was honored. It’s like he saw past the weeks of hidden disappointment I’d been hiding behind my smile, and he knew there was a story begging to be told through my dress.

With just five weeks until the wedding, I held my breath through every fitting, clinging to hope. The gown, which was for the ceremony, had a pearl-beaded lace bodice in a warmer ivory champagne. The moment I put it on, everyone—my mom, my sisters, and yes, myself—said it: “This is the one.” I never felt so sure, so complete. I felt like me: the once-shy Haitian-American girl who grew up to be a woman of the world.

And yet, we were only halfway there. For my second look, my fiancé’s entire family helped bring it to life: His brother, a menswear designer in Ghana, connected me to a talented seamstress; his sister bought the material; and his mom searched for the accessories and we discovered renowned Ampy Bridal fans. One WhatsApp call after the other, an ocean away, my gown came together: a vibrant purple and fuchsia ombré, complemented by rich kente detailing, glistening stones, and flowery embellishments.

When it finally arrived, I knew this wasn’t something even a fairy godmother could have conjured. As the first child and eldest daughter of immigrant parents, I was used to forging the path and getting things done. This time, it was a family affair, and I was catching a glimpse of who I was becoming. What started as a dress became something much bigger: a beautiful merging of my Haitian heritage and his Ghanaian roots, stitched together by the family I was becoming part of. And somehow, before we even said “I do,” I already felt like I belonged.

And then, after a dizzying two months, there I was: walking down the aisle feeling like royalty in my custom Haitian designer gown. Every emotion came flooding to the surface. The butterflies I’d been waiting for were swirling in my stomach. At the reception, I changed into my one-of-a-kind traditional Ghanaian dress, dancing towards my husband as every piece of me was on full, beautiful display: a Haitian-American, a new wife, a woman who loves a Ghanaian man and his culture. My Haitian gown let me honor where I came from; my Ghanaian gown let me honor the culture I was marrying into. And together, they felt like coming home.

#6859. You Should Be Wearing Embellished Sweaters at Your Company Holiday Party

And naturally, we’ve found the best ones.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Once again, holiday party season is approaching, and you’ve got nothing to wear. You could rewear that sequin dress that you’ve worn three years in a row, but you’re better than that. It’s time to get yourself something new for the festive season, and an embellished sweater is the perfect option.

The best part about the style is that you can take it at your own pace. You don’t need to be a complete maximalist to partake in the style, but you can be. Run full speed ahead with Cos’s sequined-out knit—a bold, glittery choice. Or, dip your toe into the glamour with a subtly beaded cardigan from Rag & Bone. And once you pick out your sweater, the fun part (and perhaps the most critical part) is styling it.

Embellished sweaters are indeed versatile, but you’ll want to balance the statement sparkle with complementary pieces. Crystal studs or pearl embellishments often pair well with a relaxed denim. Give a sequin-trimmed sweater an edgy look with a black leather legging. Embrace the luxe look and add a velvet trouser or satin midi skirt on the bottom. No matter the way you style it, let the embellishments take center stage.

Below, find our edit of the 14 best embellished sweaters to wear to your next holiday party—and beyond.

As we mentioned above, Cos committed to the full sequin style in this adorned bouclé-knit sweater. Tone it done with a simple black trouser and booties for an effortlessly glammed-up look.

Mango’s neutral sweater adds a sparkly touch to your look—whether it’s a casual jean or dressy skirt.

Endlessly versatile, you can wear this cardigan on the daily with a pair of casual blue jeans and dress it up with a satin midi skirt and kitten heels for party time.

#6858. Patrick Stewart Remembered as a Colossus of Stage and Screen

“He wore his heart on his sleeve, which made him a man to rely on, to admire and in my case, love,” Ian McKellen shared following the actor’s death.

Actor, writer, and activist Sir Patrick Stewart died on Wednesday. He was 86. In a statement issued via his social media on October 29, his family wrote that the Star Trek star and renowned Shakespearean thespian left us following “a brief and poignant journey” with Alzheimer’s disease and Lewy body dementia. “He will be remembered for the dignity, compassion, and humanity he brought to every role—in his art and life,” the statement read.

Since the news surfaced, an outpouring of grief rippled from his closest colleagues and collaborators. Notable among them, Sir Ian McKellen, a true friend and screen partner of Stewart’s for over 50 years across stage and film, wrote an emotional tribute to Stewart over social media.

“Before everything else, Patrick was defined by being born and bred in Yorkshire, the proud county in the north of England which he loved to the end,” McKellen wrote. “He wore his heart on his sleeve, which made him a man to rely on, to admire and in my case, love.”

In 1987, Stewart was already a renowned stage actor in the UK with a handful of credits in movies like Excalibur and David Lynch’s Dune when he landed his star-making role: Captain Jean-Luc Picard in Star Trek: The Next Generation. The series ran until 1994 and made Stewart a household name. Then, he starred in the summer blockbuster X-Men (2000), playing mentor Charles Xavier with McKellen opposite him as Xavier’s once-close friend turned philosophical antagonist, Magneto.

Fellow actors who worked with Stewart across Star Trek, Marvel, and beyond also penned tributes and personal anecdotes in memory of Stewart. The Next Generation co-star LeVar Burton wrote in a public statement: “He was family. There is no other context in which to hold him that makes sense. I am lucky to have known and loved so many of the facets of a soul so rich in talent and generous of heart. Of all the dames and knights I’ve known, he was deep down the silliest.”

Hugh Jackman, who starred in several X-Men installments with Stewart—including their acclaimed 2017 hit Logan—wrote on Instagram: “We lost one of the greats of stage and screen today. And, in my book, a tremendous friend and man. I learned so much from him. I will miss you, Patrick Stewart, all of my days.”

Television creator Seth MacFarlane, a noted Trekkie who cast Stewart in a handful of bit parts across Family Guy, American Dad, and his 2012 R-rated comedy Ted, said Stewart was “that rare actor who could take any bit of dialogue and elevate it, making a good writer sound great, and a great writer sound masterful.”

Wrote MacFarlane: “I remember exchanging emails with him a number of years ago when he was beginning day 1 of a new film. He commented to me that he always walked onto a new set feeling like the biggest novice there. My stunned reaction was the same as anyone’s would be. I said, ‘Patrick, if you feel that way, the rest of us are screwed.’ We have lost a legendary actor today, and for many of us, a good friend.”

Dafne Keen, who worked with Stewart extensively in Logan as a child actress, remembered the late performer on her Instagram: “He was such a kind, genuine, funny, unique, caring and charming man. I remember his endless jokes, nurturing nature and colourful reading glasses. Every single day spent working by his side was an honour and a privilege I can’t quite believe I had. He was a walking master of his craft, and of life. One of the greatest of our time, on stage and on screen.”

She continued, “I will forever hold those months we spent together close to my heart, and will always think of him as a pivotal part of how I view the world around me.”

Shortly after the news of Stewart’s passing broke, players on Star Trek Online gathered in the game’s social areas to hold a virtual vigil in Stewart’s honor.

Below, you can read more social media remembrances from Stewart’s many colleagues and creative partners, as well as theaters that were lucky enough to have Stewart grace their stages. As Jean-Luc Picard once said, “Make now always the most precious time. Now will never come again.”

#6855. For His 40th Birthday, Drake Flexed a Custom Rolex and a $250,000 Watch in the Space of 24 Hours

The rapper marked his milestone birthday with two extravagant timepieces, including a one-of-a-kind Daytona worth millions.

Turning 40 is a milestone worth celebrating in style. And to nobody's surprise, Drake did just that.

As you'd expect, the Canadian rapper's birthday outfit was not exactly understated. In fact, his watch rotation featured not one, but two rather extraordinary Rolexes. Why settle for one when you can have two?

The first was a custom, sapphire-covered Daytona, while the second was a colorful Day-Date 36 with a reported six-figure price tag. There are certainly worse ways to welcome a new decade.

First up was the Rolex Cosmograph Daytona, a watch that makes even the most extravagant diamond-set timepieces look positively modest. The one-of-a-kind creation features a diamond-pavé dial, brilliant blue sapphire hour markers, and a bezel set with baguette-cut blue sapphires. But the real showstopper is the bracelet, which is covered in a gradient of blue sapphires that gradually fade into diamonds.

And if all those precious stones weren't enough to make your eyes water, the estimated price tag certainly might. The one-of-a-kind creation has reportedly been valued at anywhere between $2 million and $5 million. Although, given that Rolex made just one, it's difficult to put an exact figure on it.

While you might assume the rapper had commissioned the watch especially for his big birthday, he actually debuted it back in August. Still, when you've got something quite this spectacular sitting in your watch collection, it would be a shame not to give it another outing.

The second Rolex was a rather different kind of extravagance. Drake also showed off a rose-gold Day-Date 36 featuring the brand's famously playful puzzle dial. Instead of traditional hour markers, baguette-cut sapphires surround a dial made up of brightly colored jigsaw pieces. And rather than displaying the usual day and date, the apertures reveal inspirational words and emojis, including a little red heart. It's about as far from your grandfather's Rolex as you can get.

The watch has been valued at around $250,000 which, rather remarkably, makes it the considerably cheaper of Drake's two birthday watches.

Of course, Drake has never been one for doing things by halves, particularly when it comes to watches. And while most people would be more than happy to receive a single Rolex for their 40th, it seems the rapper couldn't quite settle on one.

#6851. When Is the ‘Love Is Blind’ Season 11 Reunion? Here’s How You Can Watch a Full Day Early With a Simple RSVP

We’re just a few days away from the final episodes of Love Is Blind Season 11, which means we’ll find out which of our Boston-based couples will say “I do” and which will awkwardly say “I don’t” in front of...literally everyone they know.

The final episodes of Love Is Blind drop Wednesday, November 4, but considering this show was filmed in 2025 (according to fan sleuthing), we’re in desperate need of an update on where the cast stands circa right now. After all, a lot can happen in a year: the couples who said “I do” could have gotten divorced already, or even had kids. Which brings us to a very important question...

The Love Is Blind reunion airs Sunday, November 8 at 9 p.m. ET on Netflix. In other words, you don’t have to wait an entire week after the final episodes drop to find out where everyone stands.

Netflix’s fan site, Tudum, is hosting a special early screening of the reunion one day early on November 7 at 4 p.m. ET. You have to RSVP to attend the event, which will include a “pregame” where fans can chat with pod members, the reunion itself, as well as an “after party” with “never-before-seen pod dates, footage from the romantic getaways, and plenty of Bostonian accents.”

Nope! But it was filmed relatively recently!

Tudum’s event language does include the word “live” with “Expect deleted scenes, surprise guests, and a live chat where you can react to every twist, turn, and receipt in real time.” But the reunion itself was filmed ahead of time.

Per The Tab, “the reunion was filmed before the last batch of episodes premiered,” so the info will be new. Just not right-this-second new.

For what it’s worth, Love Is Blind tried to air its reunion live back in 2023, but there were technical difficulties that led to them scrapping the entire thing. But as long as the reunion was filmed recently (which it seemingly was!) we’ll take it.

See you early birds on November 7 at 4 p.m. ET!

#6844. It’s Giving Clay, Camp, and Cuteness: Inside e.l.f. Cosmetics’ New Holiday Campaign

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

These days, AI can whip up an entire holiday campaign in the time it takes you to find your favorite lip gloss at the bottom of your purse. But we are yearning for some actual making of said magic. The kind that requires human hands, dope imaginations, and a whole lot of patience.

Well, e.l.f. Cosmetics understood the assignment. And then decided to do it the hard way. On purpose.

For e.l.f.’s first-ever stop-motion holiday campaign Made to Stand Out, the beauty powerhouse traded digital shortcuts for handcrafted clay characters, intricate miniature sets, and painstaking frame-by-frame animation. It’s a nostalgic, delightfully quirky holiday tale that feels refreshingly, well, real.

The story follows three besties—Echo, Kit, and Cleo—returning home for the holidays, where old insecurities start bubbling up. But with a little help from their favorite e.l.f. beauty essentials, the trio discovers that the very things they once tried to hide are actually what make them extraordinary.

The campaign was created by Burbank-based indie stop-motion studio Apartment D Films, with cofounder Cami Kwan as the director. In an industry historically dominated by men, Kwan is helping rewrite that narrative. Women make up a whopping 90 percent of the studio’s puppet team and 100 percent of its animation team.

And because no fabulously unconventional holiday story is complete without a little Hollywood pizzazz, enter Emmy- and Tony-winning actor and The Traitors host Alan Cumming. His unmistakable Scottish voice and deliciously camp sensibility bring an extra element of joy to the project—and some giggles (”They ate! No crumbs”)!

We caught up with Alan to ask him about the project and what his greatest difference is and to dish on his favorite e.l.f. products.

It’s really lovely to partner with a brand that you genuinely align with, especially in terms of being vegan and cruelty-free. Obviously, those things are really important to me. The campaign is so authentic and artistic and really the absolute antithesis of where so much of advertising and marketing seems to be going these days. It’s lovely to be able to embrace everything a brand is saying and agree with it utterly. That doesn’t always happen.

For me, stop-motion animation is the absolute antithesis of this generic AI way we’re going. I’ve always been a fan of the art form. My friend Caroline Thompson wrote The Nightmare Before Christmas. I've known of her and her work, and the team at Apartment D and SHADOW, who worked on this campaign, were so excited that I knew Caroline and that I’d worked with her. I am so happy that every element of this project is handcrafted and created with such care and detail.

My greatest gift is my openness. I feel like I understand my differences, and I embrace them. I’m also very open to new experiences and to new people. That’s why I love this campaign message: realizing that what makes you different is what also makes you special.

People have a pretty good sense of who I am because I’ve always been so open about it. And there’s something really freeing about that. I feel seen and heard, and I think it makes people more comfortable around me, too. They know I’m approaching the world with openness and curiosity rather than a bad attitude.

I’ve somehow managed to avoid having shame in my life, and I think that’s a great gift, because it can be crippling for so many people. That’s something really lovely about this campaign. We all get little boosts of confidence from things that make us feel good, but ultimately, it’s what we believe about ourselves and our ability to embrace who we are that makes us truly great.

Yes and no. The thing is, I do run into people over the years. But when you’re famous, people see you all the time, but you don’t always see them.

It’s interesting because people from your past feel like they still know you, and in many ways they do. But they also expect you to remember them as clearly as you did all those years ago. I’ve actually reconnected with quite a lot of school friends I probably wouldn’t have if I weren’t well known. One of the lovely things about getting older and being in the public eye is that people reach out. And I’ve really, really enjoyed catching up and getting to know them in a different light.

“I use this every day. I love how it feels on the skin. Other serums can feel sort of gloopy and a bit medicinal, but this feels really like you’re putting on some sort of magical cloak. I love it!”

“This stick is great! Everyone should have more sparkle in their lives. I think everyone should dress up more!”

“I keep these in my gym bag ’cause I’m butch AF! I like moisturizing products that, again, don’t feel like you’re lathering on loads of gloopy fluid. It’s nice and light and hydrating.”

“I also love this before applying makeup. I swear nothing makes your look last longer!”

This interview was edited for length and clarity.

Julee Wilson is Beauty Editor at Large at Cosmopolitan. Previously, Julee was Beauty Director at Cosmo and Global Beauty Director at Essence and has held various editorial positions at Huffington Post and Real Simple. She counts herself lucky AF that she gets to play with beauty products for a living and tell dope stories. And if you’re as obsessed with beauty as she is, make sure to follow her on Instagram for plenty of product recs, natural hair inspo, skincare testing, and Black girl magic shenanigans.

#6840. Suki Waterhouse Just Revealed a Major Update About Her Super-Private Relationship With Robert Pattinson

After years of rumors, Suki finally set the record straight.

Robert Pattinson and Zendaya have a lot in common: They’re both Earth signs, they’ve both starred in superhero movies, they both appear in three of this year’s biggest movies (The Drama, The Odyssey, and Dune: Part Three), and—most importantly—no one could figure out whether either one of them was married...until pretty recently, that is.

It’s been a minute since Robert and his longtime love, Suki Waterhouse, welcomed their first child together (and nearly three years since they got engaged), but Suki FINALLY cleared up her relationship status with Robert—and unlike Zendaya and Tom Holland, they’re not actually married!

During a new interview with the Sunday Times, Suki confirmed that she and Robert haven’t yet tied the knot and aren’t ruling out a “massive” wedding. “It would either be all or nothing,” she said of their upcoming nuptials. “If we were going to go for it, we’d have a massive party, blow it out.”

In light of this new development, I think the time has come for a handy journey through their relationship timeline, starting at the very beginning.

Robert and Suki are spotted for the first time, and the PDA is On. Another. Level. By which I mean they’re seen kissing after a showing of Mamma Mia! Here We Go Again (wow, hot) in some pics published by E! News.

“Along the way, they were very close and cuddled and kissed many times,” an eyewitness says at the time. “Robert was very affectionate to her and had his arm around her the whole way and Suki reciprocated by holding his hand and kissing it.”

Cute, but FYI: This same month, another source downplays the relationship to E! News, saying Suki and Robert are simply “casually dating.”

The Daily Mail out of nowhere, randomly reports that Robert and Suki have broken up. Which, nope! But before long, Lainey Gossip disputes the claim, pointing out that on top of being seen together at a Miguel show in L.A., Robert and Suki both attended TIFF at the same time:

The couple is spotted leaving a Christmas party at 5 a.m. in photos published by the good ol’ Daily Mail.

At this point, Robert and Suki are getting papped a ton and are seen enjoying a stroll through London in photos published by People.

Robert is interviewed by the Sunday Times and makes it clear that he will not be talking about Suki:

“Do I have to [talk about her]? If you let people in, it devalues what love is,” he says. “If a stranger on the street asked you about your relationship, you’d think it extremely rude. If you put up a wall, it ends up better...I can’t understand how someone can walk down the street holding hands, and it’s the same as when I do it and a hundred people are taking your photo.”

He concludes with, “The line between when you’re performing and when you’re not will eventually get washed away and you’ll go completely mad.” Fair!!

Robert and Suki get papped while celebrating his birthday in photos published by People. In other words, they are Still! Going! Strong!

The pair is spotted on a double date with, um, Taylor Swift and Joe Alwyn (?!?!?!) at San Vicente Bungalows in Hollywood, where a source tells Us Weekly they “were laughing and talking” all night and “were at a table, just the four of them.” Oh, and apparently, “Rob and Taylor were across from each other,” in case you were curious about the seating arrangement for whatever reason.

Robert and Suki make a suuuper-rare public appearance together at a Dior dinner during Paris Fashion Week.

And naturally, engagement rumors are sparked thanks to that ring ^^^ on Suki’s finger.

While chatting with GQ, Robert lets it slip that he’s staying “with his girlfriend” in a London apartment during quarantine.

Suki and Robert are spotted making out on a bench, as ya do!

A source tells E! News that Robert and Suki have “definitely discussed” getting engaged but that “Suki is not pressuring him and they are both very committed to each other.”

Fans are convinced there’s a picture of Robert and Suki kissing in the background of this solo Instagram she posts:

In a since-deleted tweet, Suki criticizes the Gossip Girl reboot for using this line: “He’s R-Patz and you’re Suki Nobody.”

“Another day to be reminded that women can also be the patriarchy. Seeing critiques of the patriarchy and sexism, then I get name-checked as somebody’s nobody girlfriend,” she says at the time. “Make it make sense.”

Robert once again refers to Suki as his girlfriend in a GQ interview, saying she couldn’t stop telling a man who had come to fix the boiler about his Batman role. “I’m sitting there facing the other direction, and my girlfriend just keeps continuing the conversation with him,” he recalls. “And I’m looking at her like, ‘Shut the fuck up. Why are you doing this to me?’ She was very entertaining. Just talking to an obsessive fan.”

He also talks about Suki’s reaction to the movie during an interview with Jimmy Kimmel, saying, “It was really her reaction that kind of changed the entire thing because I’m pretty sure she’s not normally into watching superhero movies. And just seeing that it was capturing her attention the entire time and then she held my hand and touched it [to her face] and I could feel a little tear. And I was like, ‘No way!’”

After more than four years of coupledom, Robert and Suki (*fiiiiinally*) make their red carpet debut at the Dior Men Fall 2023 show in Giza, Egypt, where they are the epitome of Fashion Goals™️ (she wears a mauve dress, he wears a silver-toned jacket over a v chill turtleneck):

In addition to making their ’ship red carpet official, Robert and Suki pose for several loved-up pics at the event.

Suki makes a rare comment about Robert during an interview with The Times, saying, “I’m shocked that I’m so happy with someone for nearly five years. I had two days off the other day and it was, like, no question I’m going back to see him. Then he’ll come out for a couple of days.”

She also says, “Rob definitely isn’t getting advice from me about acting, but of course I’ll try and get him to help me with an audition before he falls asleep on the sofa.” Oh, and elsewhere in the interview, the model *also* muses on how they both grew up in Southwest London and ended up together after having such similar journeys in the entertainment industry: “We always say that I never ever would have thought I’d go out with a boy from Barnes and he didn’t think he’d go out with a girl from Chiswick.”

Suki and Robert make their Met Gala debut!

These two step out at the GO Campaign’s annual GO Gala in Los Angeles—a pretty rare appearance!

Suki confirms she's pregnant while on stage at the Corona Capital Festival in Mexico and gushes, “I’m extra sparkly today because I thought it might distract you from something else that’s going on. I'm not sure if it’s working.”

Robert and Suki spark engagement rumors yet again in pics published by TMZ of the couple walking around London and the model specifically wearing a ring on *that* finger.

But it doesn’t take long for fans to find out the truth because, not long after, a source F-I-N-A-L-L-Y confirms that the duo is engaged! “They are engaged. They both want to be married. It’s important for them,” the insider dishes to People at the time.

The celebs officially welcome their first baby!!! And according to pics published by the Daily Mail, they’re already comfortable being out and about and walking their baby in a stroller (which btw is pink 🥺).

Suki posts the first photo of her newborn baby to Instagram, writing, “Welcome to the world angel ❤️.”

That same month, the new parents show up to Coachella!

And Suki confirms on stage that they welcomed a baby girl:

During an interview with British Vogue for the outlet’s August cover, Suki reveals that she and Robert met six years ago at a game night in L.A. and notes that the activity is “one of the things people do here to congregate.”

“I was sure that I’d met him a long time ago, but he didn’t think that we had,” she begins before detailing the events of the night.

On the docket was Werewolf (“think the The Traitors, and if you haven’t seen that, then wink murder”). “It was very, very intense...There were lots of ‘big’ characters, real heavy hitters...Al Pacino was there. Javier [Bardem] and Penélope [Cruz] were there…and, you know, everyone was really acting," she adds.

Except for Suki and Rob, obvi, who found themselves drawn to each other that night. “I think we both sort of have the same slight uncomfortable-ness,” the model reveals before adding that she and her now-fiancé “started giggling at the absurdity of the whole thing.”

The two didn't end up exchanging numbers at the get-together; however, they *did* run into each other nearly a year later. And the rest is history, people!

Oh, and during another part of the interview, Suki confesses that she and Rob fully planned on having their baby: “No, we really planned it...One day we looked at each other and said, ‘Well, this is as ready as we’re going to be.’” “I was like, ‘What can make more chaos?’” she hilariously adds.

Rob and Suki attend the Veuve Clicquot Polo Classic—making their first red carpet appearance as a couple since the birth of their daughter—and ofc, they look very, very chic.

Rob gives his own take on the night he and Suki met—where, apparently, he and Suki didn’t take Werewolf as seriously as the other celeb guests would have liked. “She was sitting opposite me,” Rob says of Suki during an interview with The New York Times before confirming that Javier Bardem, Penélope Cruz, and Al Pacino were indeed all present.

He continues, “Suki and I kept making each other laugh, to the point where someone told us we weren’t taking the game seriously enough. That was a very, very sweet moment.”

A tipster writes in to gossip site DeuxMoi, claiming Rob and Suki tied the knot in the Caribbean on New Year’s Eve.

“Robert Pattinson and Suki Waterhouse got married at NYE at the Caribbean resort I work at!!!” the source claims. “They were so cute and sweet, their baby was there in a gorgeous little bridesmaids dress.”

The insider adds that “their family was there as well” and it was “such a cute wedding”—plus, Rob and Suki were reportedly “so nice.”

Soon after, Sharon Stone hosts the American Heart Association Red Dress Collection Concert, where she introduces Suki to the stage to perform and mentions that Suki gave up time with her “10-month-old baby” and her “husband” to be there, per In Touch—and apparently, Suki didn’t correct her.

Rob and Suki hit the red carpet for the Die My Love premiere and look so, so chic.

A chic night out at the Vanity Fair Oscars after-party…

…followed by date night at the L.A. premiere of The Drama! I’m obsesssssed.

Oh! And during an interview with Extra, Rob and Zendaya are asked about their “biggest secret.” Zendaya playfully teases that there’s “nothing that comes to mind,” while Rob says his big secret is “the same one that you have” while grinning at Zendaya.

Robert and Suki roll up to the Venice International Film Festival looking 10/10.

After almost two years of speculation, Suki FINALLY addresses those marriage rumors, telling the Sunday Times that she and Robert are not, in fact, husband and wife...at least, not yet.

“It would either be all or nothing,” she says of their future wedding. “If we were going to go for it we’d have a massive party, blow it out.”

Of her relationship, she also gushes, “It is almost a decade and that is a beautiful thing. It’s a thing that I go, ‘I can’t believe I have this in my life and I can’t believe that I’m so happy.’”

#6836. 3-Ingredient Curry Pumpkin Soup

This dead simple three-ingredient soup houses a wealth of flavor with a shockingly trim ingredient list. Made by sizzling curry paste in oil, then adding a can of pumpkin purée and coconut milk, this cozy soup is a celebration of simplicity, creating something that tastes far more complex than it was to make. For the best flavor, I like to use the Maesri brand curry paste, leaning on its warm intensity to lend the soup its depth, but the ubiquitous Thai Kitchen, sold in almost all grocery stores, is also nice, producing a gentle heat.

It’s That Simple is our series about recipes so easy, you can make them with your eyes closed. Think tiny ingredient lists, laid-back techniques, and results so delicious you’ll text home about them. Read more: The 3-Ingredient Pumpkin Soup You’ll Fall For.

Transfer a couple of spoonfuls or so of one 13.5-oz. can unsweetened coconut milk, stirred well, to a small bowl and set aside for serving. Heat 1 Tbsp. unrefined coconut oil or other oil in a medium Dutch oven or other heavy pot over medium. Add 2 Tbsp. red curry paste and cook, stirring constantly, until fragrant, 2–3 minutes. Add one 15-oz. can pure pumpkin purée, remaining coconut milk in can, and 1½ cups water. Increase heat to high and bring soup to a simmer. Remove pot from heat. Taste soup and season with kosher salt if needed. Ladle into bowls and drizzle some reserved coconut milk into each bowl.

#6834. Amazon’s Black Friday AirPods Deals Dropped Early

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Shop surprise discounts on the AirPods Pro 3 and AirPods Max 2 long before Thanksgiving.

While the Black Fridays of yore involved a sea of people rushing into a big-box store to score a new TV or gaming console at a record-low price, the biggest shopping holiday of the year looks a bit different these days. Now, the madness is largely confined to the internet, and the sales happen all month long, not in the wee hours when you’re fighting off a turkey-induced nap.

Case in point: Amazon’s current slate of Apple deals let you save an impressive amount on AirPods long before you’ve even finalized your Thanksgiving plans. Every time Apple gadgets, namely AirPods, go on deep discount, they tend to sell out quickly. It’s just like those Black Friday mad dashes, just from the comfort of your own couch.

Right now, both the AirPods Pro 3 and the AirPods Max 2 are on sale, although we suspect that these deals might deepen soon. If you’re looking for the cheapest possible pair, the brand-new AirPods 5 are just $129—a great deal at full price—but we predict they’ll go on sale for the first time in the coming weeks.

AirPods Pro 3 are Apple’s most advanced earbuds yet. We called them “pretty damn cool” back when they first dropped in September 2025, and more than a year later, we stand by it.

Not only do they come armed with the noise cancellation Apple is famous for, but they also boast both adaptive and transparency audio features that adjust to the environment around you. Krista Jones, senior commerce director, is partial to the adaptive mode, which she says is “slightly less noise cancelling and always tunes into my surroundings to turn things up or down accordingly.”

Jones said that the Live Translation feature has become particularly useful in her bilingual home. Heart Rate Sensing is also of note—it keeps you plugged into your heart rate during workouts without having to wear a fitness tracker.

While a fourth generation of AirPods Pro were rumored to drop in fall 2026, there’s been no sign of them in Apple’s most recent slate of releases, so if you’re looking for the most sophisticated AirPods on the market to date, these are it. We always recommend buying them when they drop under $200, but they’ve retailed for as low as $169 during other sales, so there’s the chance this price could drop more come Black Friday.

Should you prefer over-ear headphones, the AirPods Max 2 are $100 off in select colorways as part of Amazon’s pre-Black Friday deals. Back when the first generation of these dropped in 2022, editor-at-large Dave Holmes called them “Apple’s best product in years.” Four years later, Apple’s second stab has only improved the superb noise cancellation and sound quality we already loved, and added in a slate of beloved features from the AirPods Pro (think Live Translation, Conversation Awareness, and Personalized Volume), without sacrificing the sleek build.

Since the second generation was released in March 2026, the lowest we’ve seen them go is $398. Ahead of Black Friday, a $50 price cut is nothing to scoff at, especially in an age where our favorite tech has fallen prey to price increases, but you don’t need to rush either. We’ll be keeping a close watch on these.

And while the just-released AirPods 5 aren’t on sale yet, they’re still far and away the cheapest model you can get if budget is top of mind—even if you splurge on the version with a wireless charging case.

Unlike Apple’s older generations of entry-level earbuds, these have active noise cancellation, which was previously only available in premium models. They also have Live Translation and improved dust-, sweat-, and water-resistance, making them earbuds with a premium feel—sans the price tag. We’re anticipating that these will go on sale for the first time on Amazon just in time for Black Friday.

Our rule of thumb when it comes to shopping Apple tech for Black Friday? If there’s something you’ve been eyeing at a price you can stomach, it’s worth adding to your cart. When AirPods go on deep discount, they tend to sell out quickly, and then you’ll be stuck with your tired old earbuds until next year.

#6833. Kendall Jenner References Paris, Texas in a Striking Halloween Transformation

When it comes to dressing up for Halloween, Kendall Jenner loves a fashion moment. We’ve seen her transform into everyone and everything from a bedazzled Karl Lagerfeld (2015) to a feathery Victoria’s Secret angel (2018) to a golden couture fairy princess, crowned with a custom Rinaldy a Yunardi headpiece (2019). In more recent years, she’s turned her attention to films, giving her best interpretations of Pamela Anderson in 1996’s Barb Wire (2020), the Martian Girl from Tim Burton’s 1996 sci-fi Mars Attacks! (2021), and Jessie from Toy Story (2022). Teaming up with her sister Kylie, she channeled 1995’s Batman Forever in 2023 and The Lizzie McGuire Movie in 2024.

This year, she combined her love for fashion and film with a reference to the 1984 neo-Western road drama, Paris, Texas. Directed by Wim Wenders and written by Sam Shepard, the project followed Travis (Harry Dean Stanton), who emerged from the Texas desert after four years away to try to rectify his relationships with his brother, child, and ex-wife Jane. It won the Palme d’Or at the Cannes Film Festival, and has since become a cult favorite—beloved for its strange characters, raw color palettes, and eerily desolate portrayal of old Americana, as well as for that fuzzy pink boatneck sweater worn by Jane (Nastassja Kinski) during one of the movie’s most pivotal scenes.

It was this very sweater that captured Jenner’s particular attention some 42 years later. In photos shared to her Instagram, the model posed in a hot-pink, low-back minidress with the same silhouette, giving her best rendition of the recognizable character. She even tucked her brown hair into a realistic blonde wig, cut to a bob in classic Jane fashion, and had her boyfriend, Jacob Elordi, dress as Travis.

Jenner wore the look to her birthday-slash-Halloween party, where she was joined by the likes of Charli XCX, Tate McRae, and Alex Consani.

#6832. Martha Stewart’s Halloween Costume Controversy Continues After Several Apologies

Even after multiple apologies, the controversy is still following her.

Halloween has come and gone, but Martha Stewart is still facing the horrors. The famous homemaker has issued several apologies after her controversial costume raised eyebrows.

In case you somehow missed her since-deleted post, Martha seemingly dressed as an unhoused person for the holiday. She wore blue jeans, a torn-up flannel and a cardigan, and a beanie with fake dirt on her face as she held up a handwritten sign that read, “I am hungry. Help me!!”

People were equally outraged and confused by her costume choice, questioning the decision altogether.

“How do you even think this is a good halloween costume in the first place…” one person wrote, while another added, “Rich celebrities mocking poor people and their struggles is so dystopian...this is disgusting.”

Others called Martha out for being “out of touch” and “detached from reality,” as a recent report from the U.S. Department of Housing and Urban Development reveals that homelessness has increased by 27 percent between 2013 and 2025.

Upon receiving backlash for the costume, the 85-year-old multi-hyphenate released a statement to Page Six after deleting her photo.

“I am distressed that my Halloween costume yesterday, which was intended to bring more awareness to the very serious subject of homelessness, has offended people, which was never my intention,” she told the outlet on Sunday, November 1.

“Homelessness continues to remain one of America’s greatest challenges and ongoing crises, something that I care deeply about and I have spent a significant amount of time and effort supporting charities that combat this ongoing social epidemic,” she continued, adding that she is “committed to continuing to make a difference.”

If you thought the drama ended there, spoiler alert: It didn’t. After Just Jared posted on Instagram about Martha’s costume, she dropped a comment, writing, “My costume was designed to bring the homeless and the hungry to everyone's mind and boy it certainly did!!! But not in the correct way, because I did not explain it and I am so sorry I care and I support causes who care . Do all of you???”

Shortly after she commented on the post, actor, model, and activist Indya Moore shared a lengthy reply explaining why the costume was problematic.

“We all make mistakes. I certainly do. I try my best to correct them, not just with explanations but with more active support around the actual issue I may be contributing to. But this is a really tough one for me,” Indya wrote in part.

“It also seems like you apologizing for not writing a caption and our subsequent failure to understand the outfit—not because you wore it,” she later continued before calling Martha out further and sharing organizations to help the cause.

“If you were serious about poverty, you’d use your platform to back policies that tax the rich, put free food in front of people who are hungry and homeless, and provide free healthcare and education,” Indya added.

After Indya’s comment, Martha shared a series of Halloween-related content, including a “gay Halloween” post referencing a time she handed water to a child in New York City and a wholesome jack-o’-lantern pic. She then issued another Notes App apology for her costume.

“It’s hard to admit when you’ve really screwed up,” she wrote, in part. “My earlier statement didn’t go far enough. I know many people are suffering economically. Wearing a costume like I did was insensitive and not funny. I am very sorry.”

That’s all for now, but we’ll be back with more as we have it. (You know, in case there’s another jump scare in this mess.)

If you or someone you know is unhoused or are at risk of losing housing, call 2-1-1 to connect with local resources and support, including emergency shelter, housing assistance, and other essential services, or visit 211.org to find help in your area. Calls are free and confidential.

#6831. Lasagna-Vibes Zucchini Skillet

For years, one of my go-to dinners in the summer was zucchini lasagna, where zucchini is used in place of noodles. It’s delightful, but it’s also very labor-intensive, requiring you to slice the zucchini into long, thin planks, salt each individual plank, wipe each individual plank, and then layer the zucchini “noodles” with ricotta, sauce, and mozzarella. Now I have far too many children to be salting individual planks of zucchini—so I make this. It’s a dump-and-go skillet meal that has all of those favorite flavors in a way, way quicker and easier format.

Read More: Finding My Way Back to the Kitchen After Giving Birth

Heat 2 Tbsp. extra-virgin olive oil in a large skillet over medium-high. Add 1 lb. ground turkey and cook, breaking up into small pieces with a wooden spoon, until mostly cooked through with only a little pink remaining, about 4 minutes. Add 3 medium zucchini (about 20 oz.), halved lengthwise, sliced crosswise ⅛" thick, and 1½ tsp. Diamond Crystal or 1 tsp. Morton kosher salt; season with freshly ground black pepper. Increase heat to high and cook, stirring often, until meat is cooked through and no longer pink and zucchini is tender, 6–8 minutes.

Reduce heat to medium and stir in one 14.5-oz. can tomato sauce, 2 tsp. Italian seasoning, 1 tsp. garlic powder, ½ tsp. onion powder, ¼ tsp. Diamond Crystal or Morton kosher salt, and half of 1 cup thinly sliced basil leaves. Taste zucchini mixture and season with more salt and pepper if needed. Swirl in spoonfuls of 4 oz. whole-milk ricotta (about ½ cup), then sprinkle 4 oz. mozzarella, coarsely grated (about ¾ cup), over. Reduce heat to low, cover pan, and cook until mozzarella is melted, about 4 minutes.

Top zucchini mixture with remaining basil and crushed red pepper flakes (if using) to serve.

#6828. Gracie Abrams Channels Romeo and Juliet With Armor, Angel Wings, and Tons of Chanel

She put a high-fashion spin on the ’90s looks

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Yesterday evening, as people everywhere traded their typical night-out fits for spooky, theatrical costumes, Gracie Abrams dressed up for a special Halloween edition of Saturday Night Live. Serving as the musical guest for the October 31 installment, which saw Inde Navarrette as the host, the “That’s So True” star took the stage in a handful of on-theme looks.

For her two-song set, in which she performed the lead single from Daughter From Hell, “Hit the Wall,” and gave her latest release, “Here You Come, There You Go,” its live debut, the singer pulled inspiration from Baz Luhrmann’s Romeo and Juliet (1996). More specifically, she looked to the pivotal Capulet mansion costume party, where the young lovers meet and fall madly in love at first sight.

The film, which helped launch the careers of leads Leonardo DiCaprio and Claire Danes, was defined by its vibrant, modern take on the classic text, combining traditional Shakespearean language with contemporary music, fast-paced editing, markers of youth culture, and custom looks from Prada and Dolce and Gabbana. Its most famous scene saw Romeo dressed as a prince in shining armor and Juliet as an ethereal angel.

Putting her own spin on the two iconic looks, Abrams took the stage as Luhrmann’s Romeo, wearing armor layered over a ’90s Hawaiian shirt. Second, she gave Danes’s angelic Juliet a modern high-fashion update, turning to Matthieu Blazy’s Chanel for a long white silk dress. Pulled from the 2026 Métiers d’Art collection, the piece took over 600 hours to complete, and featured a low scoop neckline, delicate pleating, and intricate embroidery—with 30,000 glass beads, 2,000 crystals, and 33,000 silver sequins. The musical guest paired the piece with angel wings, of course.

Later, for the after party, she slipped into a lacy white maxi dress and slathered on the fake blood, turning into a gory vampire.

#6827. Lemon Poppy Seed Loaf Cake

Something about having a loaf of lemon poppy seed cake on the counter makes you feel like the star of a sitcom. As if you might drink your coffee from a thick-walled mug and scratch your cat under the chin just before you leave for work, a slice of said cake in your hand as you dash to the bus stop. Whether that rings true or not, know that this is a main character cake, worthy of its own show. Tall and hefty with a gentle tang and the crunchy burst of poppy seeds in every bite, it is completely unfussy yet still elegant. The bright loaf cake sings of sunny lemons thanks to the zest as well as juice. With the addition of oil and the insulating cap of snow-white glaze, it keeps nicely for a couple of days.

Find more cheerful bakes here: Chocolate Guinness Cake; Apple Cider Doughnut Loaf Cake; and Preserved Lemon Tea Cake.

Place a rack in middle of oven; preheat to 325°. Generously coat a 9x5" pan with nonstick vegetable oil spray or unsalted butter, room temperature; line with parchment paper, leaving generous overhang on long sides. Whisk 2¼ cups (281 g) all-purpose flour, 1½ tsp. baking powder, and 1½ tsp. Diamond Crystal or ¾ tsp. Morton kosher salt in a medium bowl to combine.

Beat zest of 3 large lemons, ¾ cup (1½ sticks) unsalted butter, room temperature, 1½ cups (300 g) granulated sugar, 5 Tbsp. vegetable oil, 2 Tbsp. fresh lemon juice, and 2 tsp. vanilla bean paste or vanilla extract on medium speed in the bowl of a stand mixer fitted with paddle attachment until light and creamy, about 5 minutes.

Add 3 large eggs, one at a time, beating after each addition to combine before adding the next one (batter may look curdled at this point). Scrape down sides of bowl with a rubber spatula, add half of dry ingredients, and beat on low speed just to combine. Add ¾ cup whole milk and remaining dry ingredients and mix until batter is smooth and creamy, about 1 minute. Add ¼ cup poppy seeds; scrape down bottom and sides of bowl and fold batter with spatula a few times to ensure it’s evenly mixed.

Scrape batter into prepared pan; smooth surface. (If your pan looks more than three quarters full, transfer excess batter to a greased small ovenproof container and bake alongside cake as a cook’s treat). Generously coat the blade of a small paring knife with nonstick spray; run knife lengthwise through center of batter to make a shallow cut (this will help cake split cleanly along line as it bakes rather than cracking randomly).

Bake cake until golden brown all over, it springs back when gently pressed, and a tester inserted into the center comes out with only a few moist crumbs attached, 60–65 minutes. Transfer pan to a wire rack set inside a rimmed baking sheet and let cake cool slightly in pan, about 15 minutes.

Run a small offset spatula around edges of cake to loosen, then invert out onto rack. Turn cake right side up and let cool completely.

Stir 1 cup (110 g) powdered sugar, 1 Tbsp. fresh lemon juice, and a pinch of kosher salt in small bowl, adding up to 1 Tbsp. additional lemon juice as needed, until thick but pourable. Pour glaze over cake, letting it drip down sides. Top with poppy seeds and thin strips of lemon zest if using while glaze is still wet; let sit until glaze is set, at least 45 minutes.

Do Ahead: Cake can be made 2 days ahead. Store airtight at room temperature.

#6826. The Surprise It: Welcome to Derry Episode Is Crawling with Stephen King Lore

HBO dropped a special Halloween episode over the weekend, which tells the origin story behind the origin story.

You might want to keep some of your Halloween decorations up a bit longer, because there’s a treat still hiding at the bottom of your candy bag. While everyone was out trick-or-treating on Saturday, October 31, HBO released a surprise new episode of It: Welcome to Derry. And yes, it’s scary, spooky, and very much worth watching. Especially for Stephen King lorehounds.

Titled It: Welcome to Derry — A Story of Derry, the special episode is a standalone prequel that reveals how the Sqoteawapskot locked Pennywise away in the forests of Derry. It is framed as the “real” story, unsanitized by the “gentler tale” that the Sqoteawapskot tribe cooked up for the sake of the children. “This is the story we do not teach,” reads the introduction text. “This is the part we left out.”

Kiawentiio (Avatar: The Last Airbender) reprises her role as Neekani, who previously appeared through flashbacks in season 1. But in A Story of Derry, Neekani is now the main protagonist. She defies her elders to hunt in the forbidden Western Wood and help replenish their dwindling food supply.

For most of the special, Neekani and her friends brave their encounter with “It”—also the Gallo—who is growing more powerful now that it’s feasting on English settlers. There’s also a creepy dude named Mitig (played by Zahn McClarnon) who is the first to learn that the shards of It’s giant crystal ship can ward the monster away. There’s also a predictable but nasty plot twist involving Mitig’s true motives, which I won’t give away.

But the real draw is the live-action debut of a pretty important character from Stephen King’s mythos: Maturin. He appears to Neekani after she smokes some of Mitig’s herbs, and he informs her how her tribe can contain It for at least a few generations.

A character from both It and King’s The Dark Tower series, Maturin is a benevolent turtle-like deity powered by “The White.” His goodness, purity, and light stand in opposition to It’s darkness. He has a small but pivotal role in the novel, telepathically communicating with Bill and protecting the children during the Ritual of Chüd. He dies at some point after choking on a universe. Which kind of feels like a joke on the part of Stephen King, given that turtles don’t chew.

A Story of Derry is the quickest half hour you might spend this week. It’s a solid piece of American Indian horror, which remains a criminally rare occurance throughout mainstream pop culture. (The closest I can think of is 2022’s Prey, which is barely a horror flick, though A Story of Derry feels cut from the same cloth.) There are heavy allusions to the traumas of colonization, as well as the loss of land and way of life.

While Bill Skarsgärd doesn’t appear as Pennywise, sadly, “It” puts on a scary face as a creepy, pox-riddled Christian missionary who taunts Neekani’s mother. Still, like a bar of Twix at the bottom of a trick-or-treat pail, A Story of Derry is a most welcome surprise. Now we’re left wondering when season 2 will premiere. What’s the over/under on a Christmas surprise?

#6823. These Nail Artist-Approved November Manicure Ideas Are Perfect for Cozy Season

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

I’m that person who, as soon as November 1 rolls around, is stripping my apartment of all its Halloween decor and going right into cozy season, complete with an episode of Gilmore Girls playing at all times and a warm candle burning on my bedside table. And that means I’m also thrilled to move on from my last spooky set to the November nail ideas I’ve had saved in my camera roll since, probably, August.

I might be biased (because it’s my birth month), but November is my favorite time of year for manicures. From animal prints to autumnal hues, there’s tons of chic fall nail inspiration to choose from. To find the best nail designs trending this November, I also tapped celebrity manicurists Sigourney Nuñez and John Avery for their insights and favorite nail ideas.

From our coats and accessories, down to our manicures, “animal print has been huge this year,” explains Nuñez. “Given the nature of the color palette of the Bambi nail trend, which includes a range of browns, chestnut, and beige nudes, this look will definitely be around for the next month.” You can make the look as subtle or statement-making as you want, whether that means a few delicate spots scattered across a nude base or a full set of Bambi-inspired fur.

“November is like a sweet spot for earthy and cozy colors if you’re not quite ready to commit to the festive season yet,” Nuñez explains. “Think rich espresso shades and deep greens that lean more toward emerald.” For a coffee hue, go for Manucurist’s Chestnut polish, and for some November earthiness, OPI’s Stay Off The Lawn is autumn dew, bottled.

Nothing gets me into the fall spirit like spotting colorful foliage, which happens to be peaking in NYC right now. And these detailed leaf motifs bring that same seasonal beauty to your manicure, with intricate shapes and warm, earthy hues that feel perfectly suited for November. To recreate the look, start with a milky base, then paint on your favorite leaf shapes in a mix of fall-inspired colors.

“A classic I’m loving right now is the French tip, but with a seasonal twist,” says Donna Charloff, director of service operations at nail care brand MiniLuxe. “My favorite way to freshen it up is with a deep or colorful French tip paired with a warm, creamy nude base.” Try a fall-ready hue like chocolate brown, burgundy, or burnt orange. Or why settle for just one color when you can opt for an entire striped French manicure inspired by your favorite cozy sweater?!

“The cat-eye craze is the biggest trend I’m seeing right now,” says Avery. “The magnetic gel creates movement within the color, and it can look like velvet, satin, or even a gemstone depending on the lighting.” The jewel-esque shades are especially gorgeous as the holiday season approaches, and you can do one color on each fingertip, or go for a monochrome set that still boasts some dimension.

“You can’t go wrong with a plaid design,” says Nuñez. “If you use a moody color palette with charcoals, deep purples and jewels tones, the manicure will have a dark academia vibe which feels just right for November.”

A mug of hot cocoa and November are pretty much synonymous, and the same goes for a toasty manicure. And to amp up your classic chocolate brown, try topping it off with a reflective chrome layer for an elevated autumnal finish.

Jasmine Hyman is the assistant beauty editor at Cosmo who covers all things nails—from the latest polish launches to new manicure trends. For this story, she chatted with nail artists Sigourney Nuñez, John Avery, and Donna Charloff for their insight.

Jasmine Hyman is the Assistant Beauty Editor at Cosmopolitan, where she writes about the latest beauty trends and must-have products. Her most prized beauty possessions are a meticulous skincare routine and salon blowouts. You’ll also likely find her in bed reading a good book or endlessly scrolling TikTok (spoiler: it’s usually the latter) while listening to Harry Styles’ entire discography on repeat. Follow her on Insta to be inundated with pictures of her meals.
