#6822. Chickpea Saag

This take-out staple is easy enough to recreate at home and fantastic for family dinners. If you’re looking for a comforting vegetarian weeknight meal, this stripped-down version is the ticket—it takes little time to prep, is both budget- and pantry-friendly (thanks to frozen spinach and canned chickpeas), and always goes down a treat. If you have kasuri methi or dried fenugreek leaves, crumble a healthy pinch into the spinach when you’re blending the mixture. It’ll make the saag even more fragrant and delicious. Leftover saag, if any, tastes even better the next day.

Find more delicious vegetarian dinner ideas here: Gochujang-Butter-Braised Tofu; Crispy Tofu With Peanut Sauce; and Sheet-Pan Gnocchi With Soy-Dressed Mushrooms.

Melt 2 Tbsp. unsalted butter in a medium skillet with a lid over medium heat. Add 1 large white onion, thinly sliced, 8 garlic cloves, coarsely chopped, one 1" piece ginger, peeled, coarsely chopped, 2 tsp. cumin seeds, and 2 tsp. Diamond Crystal or 1¼ tsp. Morton kosher salt. Cover and cook, stirring occasionally, until onion is translucent and tender, 8–10 minutes.

Uncover pan and add ¼ cup plain whole-milk Greek yogurt and ½ tsp. ground turmeric. Cook, stirring often, until yogurt is slightly thickened (it will split into tiny curds), about 2 minutes. Let cool a few minutes, then transfer to a blender; reserve skillet.

Add ½–1 serrano chile (depending on the spiciness of your chile and your heat preference), one 10-oz. package frozen spinach, thawed, and ½ tsp. garam masala to blender; blend until mixture is very smooth, about 3 minutes.

Return purée to reserved skillet and add one 15-oz. can chickpeas, rinsed. Cook saag over medium-low heat, stirring often, until heated through (do not bring to a simmer or saag may split).

Transfer saag to a medium bowl. Serve with steamed rice and/or warm naan, lime wedges, and more yogurt alongside.

Do Ahead: Saag can be made 1 day ahead. Let cool. Transfer to an airtight container and chill. Reheat over medium-low heat before serving.

#6819. Zara’s $70 Camel Midi Dress Is the On-Trend Design You Can Dress Up or Down

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

When it comes to shopping for chilly fall weather wardrobe staples, pieces that are super versatile, easy to style and useful for countless occasions are what we're on the look out for.

And now that fall dress season is in full swing, we need no excuse to treat ourselves to a chic piece that we'll wear on rotation all the way into spring. If, like us, you're keeping your eyes peeled for a new capsule wardrobe addition that you'll get loads of wear out of and will keep for many autumns and winters to come, we've found something you're going to want to see.

Zara's Faux Suede Midi fits the bill if you're after a piece that can be dressed up or down and makes pulling a chic outfit together so easy. Plus, with a $70 price tag, it won't hurt your bank account too much.

The faux suede dress boasts a gorgeously sleek silhouette and a stylish slit in the midi-length hem. The v-neck design and thick shoulder straps of the sleeveless number give a seriously elegant vibe. And the neutral camel-toned beige shade (one of the biggest color trends of the year, btw) just radiates timeless fall energy.

Wondering how you'd wear it? Teaming it with a standout pair of knee-high heeled boots would be our first move. Weather that be in bold black leather or rich brown suede. Or, go for a cute pair of ballet flats or streamlined trainers for something a little more casual and comfy.

This Zara offering holds plenty of layering opportunities, too. We're imagining a cosy yet lightweight cashmere rollneck worn underneath, or a chunkier oversized knit chucked on top. Add a chic longline coat, a statement scarf and your favorite winter handbag and you're good to go.

And don't rule it out for an outing that requires something on the more glamorous side. A pair of court stilettos and a cute clutch and you've got an easy go-to for an autumnal evening. Convinced? We don't blame you.
